# Performance

Median (± MAD noise band) timings, **generated by `cargo run -p x86jit-bench --release -- record`** — do NOT edit by hand. Baseline = `e11a888` "docs: rewrite the republish handoff for where the work now is", host `AMD Ryzen 7 7840HS w/ Radeon 780M Graphics / linux / x86_64`, quality `dirty` (loadavg 3.71), 15 iters. `Δ` columns compare this snapshot's min to the *previous* baseline (▼ faster, ▲ slower). The pre-push `gate` blocks a push whose jit/interp ratio regresses past `max(X86JIT_PERF_THRESHOLD` (default 10%)`, measured noise band)` vs `bench/baseline.json`, unless `X86JIT_ALLOW_PERF_REGRESSION=1`.

## Tiering — wall-clock by mode

`eager` compiles every block on first execution; `tier` interprets a block until it is hot (50 runs) then compiles it inline; `bg` compiles hot blocks on a worker thread (`x86jit-run` ships `tier`); `region-bg` (BGT-6, opt-in) tiers a hot loop up to a background-compiled superblock region — a win only on long multi-block warm loops (`hotloop`), a loss on one-shot workloads. `best↓` is the fastest mode's speedup over `eager`.

| workload | kind | interp | eager | tier | bg | region-bg | native | best↓ vs eager |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| fib32 | dispatch-micro | 1438.73ms ±1% | 101.40ms ±3% | 98.04ms | 103.15ms | 105.48ms | - | 1.0x |
| hotloop | warm-loop | 629.25ms ±1% | 43.75ms ±0% | 43.64ms | 44.49ms | 10.91ms | - | 4.0x |
| simd | simd-hot | 89.93ms ±0% | 6.21ms ±0% | 6.06ms | 6.42ms | 6.58ms | - | 1.0x |
| memcpy | stream | 125.38ms ±2% | 6.97ms ±1% | 6.24ms | 6.68ms | 4.66ms | - | 1.5x |
| indirect | indirect | 221.23ms ±1% | 32.14ms ±0% | 31.69ms | 31.66ms | 31.87ms | - | 1.0x |

_Host CPU: AMD Ryzen 7 7840HS w/ Radeon 780M Graphics. Recorded at unix 1786628748._

## Machine-path baseline (decision-23 U0)

Measured on 2026-11-11, 21:28:44–21:31:59 UTC, with engine commit
`0eb0d06d54aa196a8a26c2cdacc0a98b67e68172` and the U0 benchmark changes in the
working tree. **No engine change or optimisation.** Host: Apple M5 Pro, 18 CPU
cores (6 Super and 12 Performance), aarch64, macOS 27.0.1 (26A434), Darwin 27.0.0.
Compiler: rustc 1.98.1 (48a229cea 2026-09-01), release profile, default features.
The linker explicitly used the installed macOS 14.4 SDK because the default
linker rejected the macOS 27 SDK's text stubs.

Eight rounds per workload, four paths interleaved in rotating order. Every path
occupies each position twice. Each sample uses a fresh VM and vCPU, followed by
warm-up outside both measurement windows. The divisor is 5: warm-up is 200,000
steps, except `straight-line`, which always warms one full 30,592-step traversal
and measures three more (91,776 steps). VM construction, program/data loading,
cache fillers, state comparisons and reporting are excluded. The counter window
includes the two clock reads and counter-call overhead; no overhead is subtracted.
Wall time brackets only the measured step runner. All paths use the same runner.

`Application` is today's `set_cpu_mode(CpuMode::Real16)` path (the future alias).
`I286` and `I386` select `ExecutionProfile::LegacySystem` through
`set_legacy_cpu_model(LegacyCpuModel { generation, model_semantics_id })`, with
A20 enabled. `I286 A20 off` also calls `Vcpu::set_a20_enabled(false)`. Ordinary
hidden segment caches are seeded with `CpuState::legacy_real_entry` after setting
the selectors; changing visible selectors alone does not configure those caches.
Code, data and stack addresses stay below 1 MiB on every workload.

Every sample asserts budget exhaustion and its exact returned step count. Warm-up
and measured `retired_instructions()` counts, and final GPRs, IP, six segment
selectors, FS/GS bases, flags, vector/opmask registers, x87 registers/environment
and MXCSR, agree across all four paths and all eight rounds. All 480 samples
passed. The denominator is a `run_steps` guest unit: one completed instruction or
REP iteration. The separate completed-instruction counts below therefore differ
from steps for `dos-mixed` and `rep-movsw`.

The short probes are repeatable loops: `ip-boundary-{1,64}` branches before the
segment end instead of depending on sequential IP wrap, which is not shared
behaviour across these paths; `smc-next-{1,64}` patches the next instruction and
branches back instead of halting. The suffix is the run_steps slice budget.
`dos-mixed` retains rotating slice budgets 1/7/64/256/1024. The former cold
straight-line pass is warm-up; keeping its original four traversals also avoids
letting SI reach a segment-crossing word access. The dense-code probe retains
1,024 synthetic entries in the shared Application cache on every path; this is
not a claim that the separate legacy per-vCPU cache has the same fillers.

No builds or tests ran alongside the measurements. Process inspection (`ps`) was
blocked by the sandbox, so absence of other heavy host jobs could not be verified.
A load spike was allowed to subside before this run. `nice -n 10` was attempted,
but the sandbox rejected `setpriority`; the benchmark consequently ran at its
inherited priority. Load averages (1/5/15 minutes) were **7.63/9.58/9.75** at the
start and **9.18/7.94/8.91** at the end. The 120 pre-round readings ranged over
**3.01–10.96 / 6.72–9.58 / 8.52–9.75**. These are shared-host measurements;
retain the reported wall-time ranges when assessing the +2% wall gate.

Build and measurement commands, from the repository root. The target and output
paths are generic; the SDK path identifies the installed toolchain, not a
project-local SDK:

```sh
export CARGO_TARGET_DIR="${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/real16-loops-target"
export CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=2 RUSTC_WRAPPER=
export SDKROOT=/nix/store/ag12vlxrx98yq5cnb50wbv3ypbbfcha5-apple-sdk-14.4/Platforms/MacOSX.platform/Developer/SDKs/MacOSX.sdk
export RUSTFLAGS="-C link-arg=-isysroot -C link-arg=$SDKROOT"
nice -n 10 cargo build --offline --release -p x86jit-bench --example real16_loops
set -e
date -u '+%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ'
uptime
nice -n 10 "$CARGO_TARGET_DIR/release/examples/real16_loops" 5 8 > real16-baseline.csv 2> real16-baseline.log
date -u '+%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ'
uptime
```

The main U2 input is the size of the legacy cost: I286 uses **7.84×** the host
instructions of Application on `alu`, **6.71×** on `dos-mixed`, and **19.33×** on
`rep-movsw`; wall costs are **9.09×**, **6.99×**, and **21.70×**, respectively.
I386 is somewhat cheaper than I286 but remains far above the Direct-class +2%
target. Disabling A20 adds at most **0.17%** to I286's median instruction count
on these below-1-MiB workloads. SMC reverses the direction: legacy paths use
42–58% fewer instructions and 53–65% less wall time than Application. These are
measurements of the existing dispatchers, without attributing the gap to any
one internal operation.

These numbers establish the pre-unification Application and LegacySystem
baselines for decision-23: the alias's +0.5% per-loop instruction gate, its
`dos-mixed` +2% wall gate, the Machine-versus-alias +2% gate, the eventual alias
switch, and the unchanged-level-1 requirement when optional timing lands. Future
comparisons must use this same harness, host, build settings and sample sizes.
I8088 and level-2 overhead await those implementations. Application
Long64/Compat32 engine code is untouched; its emitted-code witness remains the
separate gate specified by the decision.

Validation passed with the build environment above:

```sh
cargo clippy --offline --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings
cargo fmt --all -- --check
cargo build --offline --release -p x86jit-bench --bins --examples
```

The 60 summary rows were also checked against all 480 raw samples, including
independently recomputed medians and identical per-path step/retirement counts.


## Direct class for Machine real mode (decision-23 U2)

This record covers U2 rebased onto `fd67927`: the I286 empty coprocessor socket
and hardware task switches. The alias reference is the provided release build
of `fd67927` with the current benchmark harness. The Machine change control is
the accepted rebased `0bdb7a4` build. U2 uses amended decision-23 §5: Machine
within +2% of Application for loops of at least 64 steps per call; every alias
loop must remain within +0.5% of the parent. `ip-boundary-1` is reported and its
Machine/alias gate remains assigned to U5. The dispatchers remain separate.

### Design and correctness

`MachineContext::segment_class` is Direct only with PE clear, no reset-fetch marker,
and six usable ordinary caches: selector-derived bases, FFFFh limits, CS attributes
9Bh and data attributes 93h. Other caches, big real mode, protected mode and reset
fetch use Checked. Direct uses shared-cache multi-instruction blocks, the common
scalar lifter and interpreter walk with Machine specialization. Generation and
width validation remains at lift time; unsupported shared forms retain the
Machine lowering. TF and architectural boundaries cap execution appropriately.

Direct keys include the Machine namespace and A20 assumption; hits validate decode
IP and context. A20-on blocks have no per-access mask. A20-off uses that memory
view where all selectors are at most F000h; upper-segment execution retains the
bus-gated, split instruction lowering. The measured loops are below 1 MiB and do
not establish shared-block speed for wrapping upper-segment accesses.

SMC invalidations select retained Checked instruction IR through a bounded
sixteen-page hint. Nearby data writes that invalidate no translations remain
shared. Source watches, publication generations, fetch permits and retained-byte
validation remain authoritative; the hint never validates code.

The paired witnesses retain whole-operand stack limits and SP addressing,
32-bit control-target checks, entry EIP validation, generation-specific vectors,
scalar MMIO RMW completion, distinct same-address string reads, source-first
Machine string fault ordering, first-missing-byte integer stores and complete
fault evidence. Segment-limit stores/RMWs retain Write intent; FP stores keep
their preflight transfer-base reporting. `PROVENANCE.md` §3 records the manuals'
silence on observable source-MMIO versus destination-limit priority. Application
string ordering and the frozen Real16 IR remain unchanged.

The parity harness compares complete exits and metadata, registers, RAM, hidden
segment/system state, x87/vector state, steps and retirement. Only private retry
identity and its deliberate unused ES attribute are normalized. ES=92h forces
Checked only while ES is not reloaded. Coverage includes RAM/Trap strings with
and without REP, I386 66h/67h forms, XLAT, far transfers, A20 wraps, stack/frame
aliases and both MMIO acknowledgments.

Task continuations retain their stage-based transfers and stay Checked. They
block Direct execution and new event acceptance. Ownership abandonment precedes
entry validation. Direct entry clears stale task completion flags; shared blocks
cannot start a task switch, and Checked transitions clear flags before returning
to Direct. The I286 first unavailable opcode byte retains priority over a later ESC MSW gate.
The verifier combines valid segment-class contexts with fourteen system-op probes;
protected-only slots remain unseen in Direct contexts. No CpuState/jit_abi fields
or frozen snapshots changed in this performance repair.

### Measured alias cost and repair

The task-switch ownership and continuation code is confined to Machine execution;
it does not run in the Application benchmark path. Relative to the parent, the
Application VM file had one change: forced inlining of `finish_single`, retained
from the earlier SMC repair. In the rebased build this enlarged the Application
resolver frame from 0x2A0 to 0x2E0 bytes and added cache-hit spill work.

Serial source bisections measured the alternatives against the supplied parent
and `0bdb7a4`, using six samples per side with divisor 5. Removing only forced
materializer inlining saved about four host instructions per block and fixed the
steady-loop failures, but left SMC-next-64 close to +0.5%. Moving all source
ownership off cache hits made the alias much faster, but exceeded the Machine/alias
gate and is not included. The final change preserves the parent's source-ownership
scope and outlines only Real16 lifting/publication into `resolve_real16_uncached`.
The materializer can still inline inside that cold frame; cached execution does
not carry its lifted IR, backend alternatives or publication drop guards.
All source watches, generation checks, fetch permits and cancellation paths remain.

| Bisection | call-ret alias | alu alias | smc-next-64 alias | Result |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Remove forced materializer inlining | 1030.445 (+0.3863%) | 399.320 (+0.2534%) | 9125.659 (+0.4893%) | Steady gates pass; SMC near the limit |
| Outline the full Real16 miss | 930.789 (-9.4141%) | 374.448 (-6.0674%) | 9083.479 (+0.0076%) | Broader hit-path change; Machine/alias gate fails |
| Outline Real16 lifting/publication | 1021.469 (-0.6904%) | 396.924 (-0.5033%) | 9117.686 (+0.2173%) | Selected cold materialization boundary |

The first cold-helper-only campaign passed every alias gate but left the rebased
Machine boundary loop about +4.03% above the alias. Machine dispatch repeated the
same continuation-state checks and reset stale task-completion flags on every
shared block. The final repair snapshots task state once per dispatch iteration,
reads saved TF only for an existing continuation, and clears stale completion
flags once at Direct run entry. Event delivery restarts the iteration; port-input
completion cannot change task state. Ownership validation remains before entry
validation, pending tasks stay Checked, and event inhibition remains unchanged.
These changes keep bookkeeping at block and run boundaries in the existing dispatcher.

Nine-sample interleaved boundary-loop bisection (I286 A20 off), with the same
alias cold helper in both candidates:

| Machine bisection | Alias | Machine | Machine vs alias |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 0bdb7a4 | 415.059 [415.027–415.177] | 425.846 [425.799–426.059] | +2.5989% |
| Reuse continuation state | 409.307 [409.296–409.373] | 418.205 [418.205–418.309] | +2.1739% |
| Also clear flags at entry | 409.369 [409.296–409.528] | 416.754 [416.646–416.911] | +1.8040% |

### Measurement method

Measured on 2026-11-12, 14:22:54–14:28:25 UTC.
Release/default-feature builds use the same host and rustc/SDK as the parent;
both builds use the same empty Rust flag set. The supplied parent binary was not
rebuilt. Five serial parent/candidate pairs alternate order AB/BA for `call-ret`,
`alu`, `dos-mixed`, `smc-next-64` and `data-next-to-code`. Each invocation uses
`real16_loops 5 3 <workload>`: fifteen samples per side and path. The other ten
workloads use three serial rounds per engine with divisor 5. An earlier divisor-1
parent ALU invocation reached the 180-second deadline under host load. The final
campaign uses divisor 5 consistently and retains every completed sample.
This parent/candidate campaign contains 1,050 samples across the five paths.

A separate interleaved three-round `0bdb7a4`/candidate comparison covers all fifteen
workloads and four Machine configurations, with divisor 5. SMC-next-1 is repeated
in five interleaved pairs (fifteen samples per side) to resolve its small upward
median. That repeated cohort replaces its initial three-sample control row.
Medians and full min–max ranges are reported without trimming. Every sample checks registers, step and
retirement parity; warm-up is excluded. No build or test ran alongside a benchmark
in this session. The host had other activity; wall time is informational.
Every cargo command used the bounded host-lock wait and at most four build jobs.

outside the repository; these tables are the retained performance record.


## Unified lowering (decision-23 U3)

### Design and scope — READ

`lift/legacy.rs` supplies one lowering for alias Real16 and Machine 16/32-bit stack, control,
flag-image, frame, far-pointer, segment-register, BOUND and XLAT instructions. Operand size comes
from the instruction; address and stack sizes come from the context. Scalar stack/near transfers
emit scalar IR; frame and segment operations use shared sized helper IR. Direct addresses inline
selector bases and constant limit/vector rules; Checked uses retained segment checks. Machine
applies A20 through its existing route; the alias ignores A20 and caches. Its helper adapter
supplies the frozen context and restores inert retained state.

Retired: `lift_call_real16`, `lift_ret_real16`, `lift_far_jmp_real16`, `lift_far_call_real16`,
`lift_retf_real16`, `lift_loop_real16`, `lift_load_far_ptr_real16`, `lift_leave_real16`,
`lift_bound_real16`, `lift_xlat_real16`, `lift_mov_sreg_real16`, the Real16 stack/flag/frame match
arms, and `specialize_shared_stacks`. Ordinary `lift_push`/`lift_pop` still serve Long64/Compat32.
Existing legacy helpers serve both profiles. Compact `LoopCx` is shared by Direct paths with a
statically word-sized count and an in-limit target; compact and general handlers call the same
width-aware algorithm. The alias's accepted forms and block boundaries remain; dispatchers and walks
are separate. Opaque helpers keep the Machine walk for evidence/context handoff.

The prefix, segment-push, far-branch and indirect CALL/JMP exclusions are gone. Remaining
`shared_instruction` restrictions have these model facts:

| Restriction | Model fact |
|---|---|
| More than ten instruction bytes | Alias keeps the 286 limit; I386 allows fifteen (80286 PRM App. C item 10 p. C-2; 80386 PRM §14.7 item 6 p. 14-6). |
| Sequential end at 10000h | Alias wraps IP; Machine checks EIP against CS (80386 PRM §14.7 item 8 p. 14-6). |
| MOV CR/DR/TR | Frozen alias has no system-register personality. |
| Dword Jcc | Alias keeps conditional-branch IP truncation; Machine checks full EIP (SDM Vol 2A §3.3 Jcc); outside these families. |
| String source FS/GS or SS | Alias lacks ordinary-string FS/GS selection and keeps the 286 #GP limit rule; I386 distinguishes #SS (80386 PRM §14.7 item 7 p. 14-6). |

The family allowlist still excludes system/numeric dispatch outside the shared scalar/string/unified
families. No unified family retains a mnemonic exclusion.

### Alias policy and witnesses — READ / MEASURED

Decision-23 §6 freezes the superset decoder, ten-byte limit, selector bases, MOV CS #UD,
word-at-FFFFh faults, in-guest delivery, no A20 and the 286 real FLAGS image. POPF/IRET clear
IOPL/NT on the alias; I386 restores its documented image (80286 PRM App. B POPF p. B-86, §5.3 Table
5-3 p. 5-7; 80386 PRM §17 POPF p. 17-130; SDM Vol 3B §25.17.2). These are model expectations, not
conflicts.

The §6 amendment accepts manual behavior within the unified families: the xj-5t9 operand-size forms;
XLAT address size and FS/GS overrides; SP wrap and whole-operand limits; POP using SP with high ESP
bits retained; whole far-frame preflight and deferred CALL/IRET commits; POPA skipping its discarded
SP slot. Word LDS/LES and BOUND with a 67h memory operand now check the **full offset** against the
alias limit, because they belong to the unified families. I286 Machine still rejects unavailable
operand-size forms; I386 is unchanged. The alias still exits on LSS/LFS/LGS and PUSHAD/POPAD through
its accepted-form boundary; Machine I386 executes them. Stack-limit faults use vector 13 on I286
real mode/alias and 12 on I386; protected stack violations use #SS(0). Direct witnesses in
`legacy_u3_review` cite SDM Vol 2A §3.3, Vol 2B §4.3, 80386 PRM §§9.1, 17.1.3, and 80286 PRM App. B
POPA p. B-85.

Outside the unified families, `SegLimitCheck::alias_low16` names the frozen ordinary-data quirk:
only the limit gate masks to 16 bits; the load/store keeps its full linear address. The single
interpreter policy point cites §6 and is consulted only when the full-offset gate fails.
Machine/unified gates use full offsets. Ordinary FS/GS MOVs retain the parent selection behavior.

`application_conflict` always returns `None`; the parity allowlist is empty. Independent
`conflict_expectation` values check both paths for segment pushes/pops, immediate dword PUSH,
near/far dword returns, dword LDS/LES, ENTERD/LEAVED, ECX LOOPcc, dword relative/indirect CALL and
indirect JMP (SDM Vol 2A §3.3, Vol 2B §4.3; 80386 PRM §§17.1.2–17.1.3 pp. 17-1–17-2).
Direct/forced-Checked parity covers these widths, SP=0/FFFFh, far frames, ENTER/LEAVE and device
reads; focused witnesses check cache/A20 independence, FLAGS, self-modification, normalized IR and
raw-proof revalidation.

The parent-compatibility table has **18 cases** with fixed GPRs, FLAGS, selectors, IP, memory, steps
and retirements: ordinary 66h/67h ALU/MOV/string forms, segment overrides, and conventional
PUSH/POP/LEAVE/PUSHF. Expectations are hand-evaluated from `24a97d0`'s `lower_read`, `with_segment`,
scalar ALU/register writes, `exec_rep_string_real` and stack handlers, independently of current-path
parity. The 67h moffs row is `67 A1 00 00 01 00`, DS=0, with 1234h at linear 10000h: parent
`effective_address_no_segment` keeps disp32=10000h, `SegLimitCheck` tests `(10000h & FFFFh)+2`, the
load reads linear 10000h, and AX becomes 1234h while upper EAX is preserved. These expectations
describe parent compatibility.

### Restart snapshots and fault evidence — READ / MEASURED

Unified scalar alias reads use the Machine ledger in retry-specialized walks; ordinary alias loads
keep their completion policy. LDS/LES, POP-to-memory and multi-read frame helpers retain each
answered transfer until one retirement. POPA/POPAD and PUSHA/PUSHAD preflight the complete frame
before any device access. ENTER stages display values before frame writes and retains completed
RAM/device displays across retries; completion clears snapshots even at the same IP.

Between an MMIO exit and resume, edits preserving ENTER's architectural inputs at their read
widths retain the attempt so no acknowledged transfer repeats; changing an input restarts ENTER.
BP/EBP is read at operand size for the initial push and, only when nesting reads displays, at stack
size for addressing; identity uses the wider applicable width. SP/ESP is read at stack size for
addressing and operand size for FrameTemp, so identity uses the wider width (SDM Vol 2A §3.3 ENTER).
Effective SS base/limit are 32-bit values (Direct uses selector<<4 and FFFFh); SS.B selects 16/32-bit
stack size. The effective limit rule and Checked read/write permissions participate as used. Identity
also includes operand size, the effective A20 boolean (always enabled for alias helpers), allocation
imm16, and nesting imm8 modulo 32. Unread GPR/segment bits do not participate. CS:IP owns the ledger.

Two identity slots retain these values exactly, without hashing. They become the first two completed
display snapshots; identity remains in unused key/high-value bits. Each snapshot retains its ordinal,
value and device-answer byte boundaries. The retained BP/EBP, SS base, stack size and operand size
determine its address. Before collection, an identity mismatch abandons all snapshots, raw answers,
frame acknowledgements and pending completions. The earlier exit owns its device evidence. This
also covers incomplete displays and attempts with no display reads. FrameTemp behavior is unchanged.

The fault-only helper-ledger projection strips visits/write tags, suppresses internal identities and
snapshots, and expands display provenance into the original bus-address/value pairs. Its shared-walk
caller selects actual legacy helper IR; ordinary scalar/vector instructions export their original
ledger keys, including alias linear addresses above FFFFFFFFh. Its dedicated legacy instruction-walk
caller projects that walk's helper keys after either scalar or opaque fault analysis. Witnesses cover
alias, Machine Direct and Checked ENTER, far CALL/RET, IRET, POPA, LDS and BOUND faults, plus ordinary
MOVDQU with an acknowledged read at 1_0000_0000h. Non-ENTER helper faults now report completed
device reads, where the parent reported none.

Only the current attempt is retained. Before two displays complete, two identity slots plus at most
four byte answers need six slots. Later collection peaks at 29 snapshots plus four answers (**33**).
Zero/one-display attempts retain two identities and at most four write parts (six). Otherwise the two
identity slots are already among the 30 snapshots; 32 frame writes, or 33 when one crosses an
A20/32-bit bus boundary, give **63 of 64** slots. No phase reaches the ledger's release overflow drop.
Maximum-nesting witnesses, including abandoned attempts, run in debug and release. Other witnesses
cover overlapping EBP edits, ignored upper register bits, ESP FrameTemp edits, word/dword stacks,
SS limit/allocation edits, BP/SP/SS/A20 edits, device-page splits, and 65 frame-only input edits.
Only ENTER executes identity checks and snapshot collection/lookup; projection runs on faults.

### Snapshot changes — MEASURED

| Fixture (backend paths under `x86jit-cranelift/tests/snapshots/real16/`) | Final change |
|---|---|
| `stack.txt` | Shared scalar stack/near-CALL IR, sized selectors, constant stack checks. |
| `far-call.txt` | Shared sized far-transfer helper. |
| `flags-all.txt` | Sized FLAGS/all-register helpers, original block boundaries. |
| `segments.txt` | Shared segment-load helper and ordinary `alias_low16: true` gates. |
| `memory.txt`, `sizes.txt`, `prefix-combination.txt` | Explicit `alias_low16: true` on ordinary data gates. |
| `x86jit-tests/tests/fixtures/real16_parent_ir.snap` | Stack entry uses shared lowering. |

The other two Real16 backend fixtures are unchanged. All 27 parent/candidate Long64/Compat32
block/region IR captures match byte for byte; Application codegen also matches 27 relocated fixtures
and four fixed byte sequences. No Application fixture or backend source changes. This evidence
repair changes no IR fixtures.

### Performance — MEASURED

Release builds use matching Rust 1.98.1 compiler stamps, the supplied SDK and empty Rust flags.
Commands use `taskpolicy -c background`, one benchmark at a time, without own builds/tests
alongside. The alias recheck uses divisor 1 and three serial warmed invocations per loop: 45 samples
per binary. Each invocation stops after its completed alias sample; all counts and medians are
verified. No steady loop executes ENTER, so this gate does not measure ENTER's path cost. Wall uses
five interleaved warmed alias `dos-mixed` pairs: 1,000,000 warmup steps, 8,000,000 measured steps
and 7,338,677 retirements each.

## Alias switch and shared interpreter (decision-23 U4+U5)

**MEASURED (final round):** alias +0.5% PASS; Machine +2% FAIL on `ip-boundary-1` only (+2.56 % to +2.63 %, landed with the maintainer's acceptance: decision-23 Performance requirement, landing amendment; bead xj-s3p.11); dos-mixed wall +2% PASS; Long64 PASS. The abandoned protected REP flag-origin defect is fixed. The full combined rollback corpus and all required runtime checks pass.

**READ:** the accepted U5 amendment (2026-11-13) gates Machine against the pre-switch U3 alias. Machine cells report **vs U3 alias first / current alias second**. The frozen U3 executable lacks `smc-rmw-64`; its reference uses the identical standalone driver linked against the saved U3 library. No gate is relaxed.

### Design and guards — READ

`run_dispatch` remains the sole outer loop, with private profile constants. `interp::walk_ops` remains the sole opcode iterator; `interp::legacy::execute_op` handles one system opcode and transactional slices use `InstructionSlice`. The alias remains frozen `REAL16_ALIAS`: M4/M5 behavior, selector-derived caches, InGuest delivery and its decoder/quirks. No public API or CPU/jit_abi layout changed; Long64/Compat32 lowering, keys and backend codegen are unchanged.

The lifter still emits `LegacyOp::Checkpoint` only for a CPU/flag/helper commit before a later fallible operation. Only that mark takes the per-instruction rollback snapshot; task partial commits and helper evidence retain their own rules. Scalar RMW flags commit after the store in both Direct and Checked lowering, including retained SMC. No Direct scalar rollback snapshot is reintroduced; the conservative Checked coprocessor gate/body witness remains.

Shared partial-block `BudgetExhausted` completion now uses the existing fast `Progress` carrier when MMIO ownership is inactive and no port-string state needs deferred cleanup. Live MMIO and port-string cases retain the outcome path; TF still selects post-instruction debug handling. The new `partial_shared_budget_completion_keeps_mmio_cleanup` witness exercises an authoritative multi-instruction block after a completed device read, both models: retirement once, cleanup, correct RIP and the next budget slice. Machine uses the existing unstepped dispatcher specialization; its unchanged instruction limit comes from `initial_context`. The public `run_steps` pending-IN check still uses the outer stepped profile. `finish_machine_run` skips a proven no-op budget cleanup only when MMIO ownership is inactive, task/instruction continuations are absent and no A20 change is pending. Other stops and owned continuations keep masking, retry cleanup, A20 application and task sealing.

A private `SINGLE` specialization is selected only for an explicit Machine budget of one. Its limit/cap is exactly one, so it does not calculate a second cap from TF/shadows/IRQ/A20/readiness. Those inputs still control debug/event delivery, inhibition, IRQ readiness and queued-A20 application. Its pending-I/O predicate is queried live whenever an asynchronous host request needs eligibility; the normal multi-step descriptor remains unchanged. Budget priority, per-block host polling and SMC/fetch authority remain live.

Warm entry returns a small `Option<MachineContext>`; the caller invokes the existing cold `Result` path only when the same warm guards fail. Stamp comparison, epoch/model, public CPU edits, continuation/shutdown, RIP and task-debug checks retain their original conditions and order. This changes the private return carrier, not validation. The validated entry also caches `raw_direct`: its only inputs are effective A20 and the six visible selectors, all covered by the full stamp. Successful cold preparation computes it; warm entry reuses it only after that stamp matches. Existing in-run refresh calls remain after segment/context changes, event delivery and A20 application. `cached_direct_bus_revalidates_selectors_and_a20` pins masked/unmasked loads across public selector/A20 edits on both models.

The full initialized-field stamp comparison is retained, including reserved fields and the original CR2/output-flag exclusions. No fingerprint or padding read is used. Boundary descriptors refresh after helpers, faults, port completion and event delivery; ordinary shared completion can reuse them only while inputs remain fixed. New inhibition, pending I/O and one-step conditions force refresh. Context-qualified Arc classification still follows the authoritative VM cache/guest-start checks and clears on changed entry/context, faults, events, reset or translation invalidation.

### Attempt ownership correction — READ / MEASURED

The protected wrapper keeps the SDM original-EFLAGS rule: Vol 2B §4.3 REPE/REPZ p. 4-565, REPNE/REPNZ p. 4-567; Vol 3A §§7.5–7.6 describes fault restart. Completed count/indices remain committed. The flag origin is a private Flags memento, separate from the lift-marked CPU rollback snapshot. Qualified IR still carries the cached Protected walk choice; fixed alias and validated Direct classification omit its impossible regime predicate. No IR, key, backend, public API, CpuState or jit_abi layout changed.

Round 6 confused reusable translation identity with a live instruction attempt. A CS-limit or CR0 edit could produce a fault outside the protected wrapper, leave its origin retained, then match the old Arc/stamp/cursor/flags/retirement after the original context returned. The supplied fetch witness reproduces stale FLAGS=0803h instead of the new attempt’s 0046h; all 48 CR0 round trips fail. Both supplied witnesses pass when linked against 36a9703 and fail against the exact saved R6 library.

`run_engine` now encloses the unchanged run body in a private entry helper, so every normal return (including preflight errors) reaches one ownership boundary. For a nonzero Machine call, that boundary **takes** the retained origin and returns it only for an explicitly allowed continuation whose inputs still match. All faults, refusals and unlisted/future exit kinds consume it by default. This is linear ownership, without a reusable numeric identity or a new list of clearing sites. A retained context/Arc can never reconstruct a consumed origin. Zero-budget probes remain outside an instruction attempt and do not consume it.

The wrapper and ownership boundary share `RepFlags::matches`: model/full context stamp, instruction IP, count/SI/DI/AX, current Flags/system flags and retired count. The wrapper additionally checks the qualified Arc. Edits that reach the wrapper start a new origin; edits exposed by a return before the wrapper fail the boundary guard. Within a run, context-changing instructions retire or pass the non-comparison wrapper; accepted events already consume the origin before delivery. Task helpers retain their existing partial-commit rules.

| Return / transition | Origin ownership rule | Witness / audit |
|---|---|---|
| Budget exhaustion, host interruption, IRQ-ready yield | Return only with matching attempt inputs | Existing step slices; `host_stop_preserves_only_the_unchanged_comparison_attempt` |
| MMIO read/write | Matching inputs **and** active owned retry at this IP | Existing comparison MMIO chain; answered-comparison retirement matrix |
| Fetch/decode/map/architectural fault, unsupported context, shutdown, pending-IN refusal, every unlisted exit | Consume; a later context round trip cannot revive it | New fetch/entry matrices; redirect/SMC probes; common result boundary covers all returns |
| Retired instruction, different instruction, accepted event, reset, changed code/input | Existing wrapper/event/reset abandonment plus the common boundary | Retirement count/Arc guards; IRQ-origin test; reset/SMC/context-churn probes |
| Zero budget | Preserve without consuming execution/completion ownership | `zero_probe_keeps_origin_but_reset_drops_it` |

All names below are in `legacy_protected::comparison_attempts`. Eight review probes were adopted; three additional tests cover the same hole before wrapper entry and both public run APIs. The added entry/mapping/edited-host-stop tests also fail against the saved R6 library.

| Contract / cases | Witness | R6 before fix | R7 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hidden CS.limit fetch #GP, restored context | `comparison_origin_is_not_reused_after_hidden_cs_fetch_fault` | FAIL: 0803h instead of 0046h | Pass |
| CR0 round trips; address/operand 16/32 × CMPS/SCAS × REPE/REPNE × budgets 1/2/8 (48) | `comparison_origin_is_not_reused_after_cr0_round_trip` | FAIL: 48/48 | Pass |
| Unmap/remap fetch, run/run_steps × budgets 1/2/8 (6) | `comparison_origin_is_not_reused_after_fetch_mapping_round_trip` | FAIL | Pass |
| Unsupported paging context and pending-IN StepRunError (2) | `comparison_origin_is_not_reused_after_entry_rejection` | FAIL on the first unsupported-entry case | Pass |
| Host stop: unchanged or edited CR0/CS.limit/CF/count, run/run_steps (10) | `host_stop_preserves_only_the_unchanged_comparison_attempt` | FAIL on first edited case; unchanged case passes | Pass |
| Redirect to a different faulting instruction | `origin_is_abandoned_after_an_observed_different_instruction_attempt` | Pass | Pass |
| Changed invalid opcode and restored bytes at the same IP | `origin_is_abandoned_after_invalid_smc_at_the_same_address` | Pass | Pass |

### Rollback sweeps and retained review coverage — READ / MEASURED

`legacy_rollback_sweep` now runs the complete supplied Code32 dataset through the existing corpus helper, preserving the original real-mode dataset. No subset was cut and no environment or trace switch was added. Counts are pinned, and failures name bytes, mnemonic, memory case, stop, and before/after GPR/flags/system/selector/hidden-cache state. Completed device evidence and coprocessor state remain outside the reviewed comparison.

The brief’s 38,256 count is the combined corpus; the supplied Code32 loop alone enumerates 19,062. This was measured before fixing the engine as well. Full debug/optimized binary runtime: **1.59s/0.36s**, both within 180 seconds, two test threads. The exact same source linked against the saved U3 library passes both sweeps.

Round-6 `dispatch_guards` probes remain: full Code32 ECX/ESI/EDI above FFFFh with run/run_steps parity; queued A20+IRQ during owned MMIO including zero-budget probes; Code32 POPFD/TF and a public TF clear. Their public I386 fixture still requires interpreted execution. The review worktree was read only.

### xj-srh.5 folded into U4+U5 — READ / MEASURED

The original audit left combined contracts unpinned. The expanded matrix found one comparison-fault flags defect; the other requested contracts already worked. Thirteen round-5 tests in `legacy_protected::stepping_contracts` run the Code32 matrix under I386, with a panic backend enforcing interpreted execution. The catalogue-model wrapper repeats all twelve semantic witnesses through `set_cpu_model`, with outward fault delivery; the other leg uses the legacy selector. The first eight contracts pass against round 4; the comparison-fault flags witness and its MMIO chain fail before the fix and pass afterward. That round-5 stepping-contract test source was separately linked against the saved round-4 release library: 10 passed / 3 failed (the main flags case, the MMIO chain, and their catalogue-model wrapper). Hardware task switches, V86, DR/TF debugging and 386/387 integration retain their existing witnesses and behavior.

The pinned SDM 325462-092US numbers stack-switch inhibition **Vol 3A §7.8.3** (the brief calls it §6.8.3). Rules were read from the main oracle stash: Vol 2A §3.3 IN/INS/CALL/IRET/JMP; Vol 2B §4.3 REP/MOVS/CMPS/STOS/LODS/SCAS/STI/MOV/POP/OUT/OUTS/RET; Vol 3A §§7.5–7.6, 7.8.1, 7.8.3, 7.12.1 and 12.9.1–12.9.2. 80386 PRM §17 REP pp. 17-139–141, STI p. 17-163, §9.2.4 pp. 9-3–4 and §§14.4.1–14.5 p. 14-4 are second witnesses.

Names below are in `legacy_protected::stepping_contracts`; “Pass” is the result before any runtime fix, against round 4. The first eight rows need no runtime change; comparison fault restoration needs the flags origin fix. The harness initially needed correct region-sized mappings and writes confined to each region; those were test setup errors, not engine failures.

REP matrices cover default DS and 66h/67h independently and together, FS source override (SS for #SS), both DF directions for successful memory strings, and effective count/indices. Fault matrices use budgets 1, 2 and 8; exact-completion memory-string slices use 1, 2 and the remaining N (at most 3); the combined shadow/REP test uses literal 1, 2 and 8. Empty REP retires once; a partial REP has not retired. Each fault preserves both committed destination elements and the untouched third RAM element where observable. Port matrices assert that repeated ready probes add neither units nor handoffs; OUT/OUTS are acknowledged by resumption, IN/INS by the existing completion API.

The R6 artifacts are fixed copies from the previous campaign; no diagnostic bypass or baseline rebuild is substituted. ### Frozen real-mode comparison-fault witness — MEASURED / READ

`legacy_rep_fault_flags::repeated_comparison_fault_flags_match_frozen_real_behavior` covers 84 cases: CMPS/SCAS × REPE/REPNE × budgets 1/2/8, on Machine I286/I386 and alias. The identical diagnostic linked against 36a9703 and the final library produces identical rows. Arithmetic-mask original flags are REPE 0801h / REPNE 0841h; the last successful comparison leaves 0044h / 0000h. Fault cases commit two elements, retain count=1, EIP at the prefixed instruction and zero retirements.

| Path/case | At 36a9703 | Now | SDM rule / result |
|---|---|---|---|
| Machine I286, unmapped memory, address16 | Last comparison flags | Same | Original EFLAGS required for comparison faults; engine-map analogue is decision-16 §3 |
| Machine I386, unmapped address16/32 and address32 operand past FFFFh (#GP) | Last comparison flags | Same | Original EFLAGS required; rule unmet |
| Alias, unmapped address16/32 | Last comparison flags | Same | Original EFLAGS required; frozen behavior records the gap |
| Alias, address32 operand past FFFFh | Completes third element and HLT; no limit fault | Same | No fault restoration observed; real-address limit behavior also differs |

SDM Vol 2B §4.3 REPE/REPZ p. 4-565 and REPNE/REPNZ p. 4-567 state the original-EFLAGS rule; Vol 2A §3.3 CMPS and Vol 2B §4.3 SCAS define real-address operand-limit exceptions. I286 has no address32 case. Alias limit completion has count=0, four units, two retirements and EIP after HLT. The witness deliberately asserts current behavior and points at the rule. No real-mode or alias behavior is fixed here; the supervisor will file the separate real-mode bead.

### Remaining — MEASURED / READ / ASSUMED

|---|---:|
| ip-boundary-1 | 14.314…16.057 |

The complete R6/R7 one-step control adds 18.303–22.929 instructions/call. These are real-mode runs with an empty origin, so the new full matching/take-return helper is not entered. The net cost includes the empty-origin test and generated result/entry code; their individual instruction contributions were not isolated. Register allocation/result-carrier explanations remain ASSUMED. Closing the listed residual needs specialization or reuse of an existing boundary while keeping every preflight and dispatcher return inside the fail-closed ownership rule. No semantic guard may be dropped.

A final gate fails; code is retained and work stops. The protected abandoned-origin defect is fixed. Real-mode/alias differences stay documented for the separate bead. No public API/layout or IR/backend fixture changed. Decision-23 was read, not edited. Its supervisor-owned ordering amendment changed concurrently; the measured U4/U5 gate text did not. No commits, git writes, bead operations or pushes were performed.

### Decode design and identity — READ

`model/decode.rs` contains the explicit encoding membership table (one bit per
current model), compiled to a constant-time code-indexed array. Decoder feature
tags no longer decide Machine membership. Each `DecodeSet` owns decoder options,
its segment-prefix policy, and the absent-encoding result. `DecodeAction` separates
a defined instruction, a canonical alias, and absence: `Some(vector)` emits a
fault, `None` returns a lift refusal which becomes `Exit::UnknownInstruction`.
Both current entries select vector 6; neither uses the refusal policy. This
provides the C2 policy without creating an 8086 model or guessing its encodings.
The incremental 286 rejection is shared by fetching and lifting, preserving
fault priority before later bytes or coprocessor gates are examined.

SDM Vol 3B §25.15 pp.25-5–6 licenses canonical 82h group-1, group-2 /6 and
group-3 /1 aliases. The existing accepted aliases retain their results. Reserved
`UD0`/`UD2`, later NOPs and identity instructions are absent from the explicit
sets; `UD1` is now absent too. The 80386 PRM Appendix A two-byte opcode map
p.A-7 leaves 0F B9 undefined, and §14.6 Table14-1 p.14-5 requires #UD at the
faulting instruction. This fixes the baseline's I80386 refusal. Existing
reserved LOADALL/XBTS/IBTS slots continue to fault; the table does not admit them.
(A later note: xj-oog makes 0F 05 LOADALL on `I80286`, spec.md §17.10. This
record describes the decode before it.)
The older unsupported coprocessor pairing policy is preserved (decision-25).

`ModelQuirks::flags_fixed_zero` names AC/ID as catalogue data. Both entries
select the existing early-processor flag helper shape at lift time. The central
flag-load mask also names this same catalogue constant; it adds no per-op model
lookup or branch. POPF/IRET and task switches already cleared AC/ID at the
baseline, and flag/interrupt/task images already excluded them. This is a
catalogue migration and new witnesses for that behavior, not a claim to repair
an AC/ID toggle defect that the baseline witnesses could not reproduce.
The other three quirk fields were unused at the baseline. They now select the
supported lowering shape at lift time: pre-decrement PUSH SP value, five-bit
counts, and the existing real-mode FLAGS image. Unimplemented shapes remain
lift refusals; C2 supplies its own lowering. No quirk field is read by the
interpreter. Existing runtime generation checks in system helpers remain listed
in the inventory and are not expanded here.

Identity authority, checked in the pinned local PDFs: SDM Vol 3B §25.13.1 and
Table25-1 pp.25-4–5, §§25.15–25.16.1 pp.25-5–7; 80386 PRM §2.3.4 Fig.2-8
p.2-11 and §14.6 Table14-1 p.14-5; 80286 PRM §2.3.4 Fig.2-10 p.2-15,
§5.2.3 Table5-2 pp.5-5–7 and Appendix B #UD p.B-12. The flag diagrams and #UD/
opcode-map page images were checked, not only OCR. The SDM instruction pages
are Vol2A §3.3 BSWAP p.3-111, CMPXCHG p.3-194, CPUID p.3-203, INVD p.3-483,
INVLPG p.3-485; Vol2B §4.3 RDMSR p.4-544 and RDTSC p.4-559; Vol2D §6 WBINVD
p.6-2, WRMSR p.6-8 and XADD p.6-27. The latter three are in **Vol2D** in the
pinned SDM. No second-witness inference from an emulator was used.

`semantics_id` uses deterministic FNV-1a over explicit encoding membership and canonical alias pairs,
decoder/absence/prefix policy, quirks, system/physical/length attributes, lift
constants and FPU/timing selection, prefixed by `LIFT_POLICY_REVISION`. Changes to
validation or alias/lowering rules independent of those values must bump that
revision. Current identities are I80286 `3699e02a8ef13e63` and I80386
`e8d00c17f97eb71f` (previously 286h/386h). Checked cache keys and context stamps
include the identity, so old entries cannot hit. Direct caches are owned by a
VM with its model locked before execution; no persisted cache survives a library
rebuild. No Application cache-key/hash stream changes.

The public change is one additional named attribute on the already non-exhaustive
`ModelQuirks`, required by decision-23 §2's identity catalogue. The explicit decode
sets, disposition and lift-policy details are private. No new CpuModel variant,
Exit variant, public method, IR variant, dependency, CpuState field or jit_abi
layout is introduced. Long64/Compat32 and the frozen Real16 alias retain their
existing decoder. The architecture and spec §17.10 describe the new consumers.

### Round-2 differences retained — READ

- I80286 physical width: `physical_bits=24` is catalogue data, while the bus
  helper wraps formed addresses at 32 bits and applies the separate A20 gate;
  it does not generally truncate to 24 bits. Authority: 80286 PRM App.C hardware
  item2 p.C-3; §6.3 Fig.6-3 p.6-5.
- I80286 LOCK: the strict Machine decoder rejects forms which the 286 permits
  with MOV, MOVS, INS and OUTS. Authority: 80286 PRM App.C item11 p.C-2;
  80386 PRM §14.8.1 p.14-8. The alias's permissive decoder is frozen.
- I80286 x87 operand beyond the segment end: integer-style address checks select
  #GP/#SS instead of the 286's interrupt9. Authority: 80286 PRM App.C TableC-1
  p.C-1. I80386 Machine selects #SS/#GP through its existing address path, as
  required by 80386 PRM §14.6 Table14-1 p.14-5. The generic `state.rs` divergence
  comment describes alias behavior and needs a separate documentation audit.
- The complete model-specific coprocessor and exception sets are not supplied
  by `FpuModel::Present` (it deliberately makes no pairing-generation claim).
  Generation-specific numeric-error details and unsupported x87 forms remain
  outside this round. This is a coverage limitation, not a measured wrong-vector
  result for the numeric-error row, whose implemented present-x87 path uses #16.

For the 286 sequential-IP row the decision itself says “to derive”; there is no
licensed value here against which to call the current limit fault a mismatch.
The already-recorded real-mode REP comparison-fault differences in U4/U5 remain
unchanged and are outside §3's quirk table.

A further **READ source conflict** belongs to the undefined-encoding row: the
80286 PRM Appendix B #UD p.B-12 names F6h /1 as an invalid extension, whereas
SDM Vol3B §25.15 p.25-6 defines group3 /1 as the TEST /0 alias for compatibility
with prior generations. The baseline executes TEST on I80286, and C1 preserves
that behavior and pins it under the SDM rule (PROVENANCE.md §1 names the SDM as
the instruction-semantics authority). The 286 PRM therefore does not corroborate
this specific alias. A model-specific resolution belongs in the supervisor's
round-2 brief; this report does not silently claim the sources agree.

also start with dirty system-flag storage and verify it is cleared on load.

### Counted-gate method — MEASURED

Rust 1.98.1, macOS AArch64, SDK14.4/deployment14.0, background scheduling,
at most four Cargo build jobs. Baseline built from 7274dd96, before source edits;
candidate built with the same flags. One benchmark process at a time, with no
own builds/tests overlapping counted runs. Each of all sixteen workloads uses
literal `real16_loops 1 3 <workload>`, including `smc-rmw-64`. The final candidate is compared against the unchanged, frozen three-round baseline measurements from the initial campaign. Cells below are
median host instructions/step, baseline→candidate (percent change). All five
paths' step, retirement and warmup counts must agree before a result is accepted.
No timed sample is filtered. The C1 gate is +0.5% against the corresponding
7274dd96 path, including Machine; the historical U3 gate is not substituted.

Initial-campaign executable SHA-256: baseline
`3d4749ef669f4c97656cbcba96d3d256d2265f15c865cdec1c6f68fe1ae48e5f`;
initial candidate `16335e0502f7016a9a341391370611f0f6e090ebca57bc7890a0d947121ae29a`.
The baseline hash also matches the frozen U4/U5 R7 executable above.

### Initial SMC control and classification change — MEASURED / READ

The first full campaign failed six I80386 SMC cells: `smc-next-1` +0.777% /
+0.751%, `smc-next-64` +1.147% / +1.150%, `smc-rmw-64` +1.160% / +1.185%
(A20 on/off). The other 74 loop cells and both store widths passed. These
samples are retained; no failed sample was filtered. The initial candidate is
the executable with hash `16335e05…` above.

The revised classifier checks membership first, uses a const-derived marker to
bypass the canonical-alias match for ordinary instructions, and limits 286
prefix/Sreg checks and memory-only table checks to the relevant admitted forms.
Quirk/helper-shape checks now run only for their instruction families. This
narrows lift-time work while retaining the same current-model results and fault
policy. The semantics digest also directly hashes canonical alias pairs. The
source-level extra checks were identified by inspection; no precise assembly
attribution is claimed for the six measured increases.

A second focused control (`b8f8cb86…`) still failed `smc-next-1`: alias
+0.501%, I386 A20 on/off +0.940%/+0.897%; I286 on/off passed. That campaign
stopped at the failed workload; its measurements are retained separately and
are not mixed into the final candidate table. Disassembly then showed that
Machine I386 still entered the out-of-line 286 prefix checker, including its
stack frame, where the original generation predicate had skipped it. The
catalogue's prefix capability now guards the call in both fetch paths. Quirk
checks sit in their corresponding match arms rather than in a shared prologue.
The complete net effect is measured by the final gates; the six initial deltas
are not assigned wholly to that single call.

## C2 I8088 and I8086, round 1 (baseline d4a58e2a)

**READ:** the catalogue adds `I8088` and `I8086`: 8086 ISA, `system = None`,
20 physical address bits, no instruction-length limit, `None`/`Present` FPU
policies and no timing table. The CPU encodings come from
`oracles/intel/i8086-um.pdf` (9800722-03), §4.2 Tables 4-12/4-13 pp. 4-22–35
and Table 4-14 p. 4-36. Decision-23's accepted amendment admits 82h as 80h and
F6h/F7h /1 as TEST /0 (SDM Vol 3B §25.15 pp. 25-5–6, corroborated by the
"alias" entries in `oracles/ss8088/v2/metadata.json`). D0–D3 /6 stays refused
("undocumented"). Other absent forms, later additions and the 0F map return
`UnknownInstruction`, with no fabricated #UD. LOCK also remains a typed
handoff: UM §2.5 pp. 2-17–18 requires whole-instruction bus ownership, including
REP. AAM/AAD accept only the second byte 0Ah printed in Table 4-12 p. 4-24.

Both models execute in a permanent Direct context. Their instruction results
are identical; Appendix B pp. B-31–32 describes bus, prefetch and timing
differences, including four- versus six-byte queues. These differences are not
modelled at this timing level. Spec §17.10 records the quirk witnesses, REP
restart rule, deterministic undefined-flag values and FPU policies.
**ASSUMED engine policy:** WAIT with `None` sees ready TEST. `Present` is the
existing generic x87 engine, without an 8087-faithfulness claim; C6 supplies that
pairing. The C3 hardware differential lane was not run.

**MEASURED validation:** 41 runtime binaries, 1,395 passed, five ignored, zero
final failures, including all 21 `legacy_*` binaries, 421 core tests and 27 C2
witness tests covering both models. Boundary/environment, run_steps, IRQ shadow,
retirement, SMC, fences and Application code-byte witnesses pass. All 36 captured
Application/alias IR images are byte-identical to C1; all 27 C2 release witnesses
also pass. Clippy across all targets/features with warnings denied, fmt checking,
and the workspace release test check with Unicorn pass. An earlier fence run
timed out during a build; its log is retained, and the final isolated binary
passes all six active tests. Direct-rustc fixtures use the same SDK linker wrapper
as Cargo.

**READ / MEASURED provenance:** cited values were checked against page images at
oracle revision `c591d7a`. The hash-checked 8086 UM SHA-256 is
`717194ec03520d6499a40523abdfd4ed15a2b8ccc45a21c653e2b443c248664c`;
the iAPX 86/88,186/188 PRM SHA-256 is
`7f33bead2b7111b6871b50f14368531b90c30d960ca47865c5b0d8aa5fb55ed2`.

**MEASURED method:** Rust 1.98.1, native macOS arm64, SDK 14.4/deployment 14.0,
standard release settings, empty Rust flags. Background scheduling, at most four
build jobs, 30-minute build limits, and 180-second individual test/benchmark
limits. No build or test overlaps counted runs; only one benchmark process runs
at a time. Each workload uses literal `real16_loops 1 3 <workload>`; values are
three-sample median host instructions per step, with equal warmup, step and
retirement counts. All 336 samples pass the guest-state/accounting assertions.
Earlier failing controls are retained separately without filtering or resampling.

The retained d4a58e2a baseline executable SHA-256 is
`4db0da02d946edc96164f2b70b8479c507b363f0482a4542eb1a601debe9c461`;
the final C2 executable SHA-256 is
`49a6a0185385148a25d6c5416fecf9b3e407d8ce74c62721b39a9243015df7a0`.
The existing dispatcher keeps its original four walk kinds; Direct20 selects
its shared specialization separately. Basic accounting counters publish
separately from optional detailed storage. SMC avoids a coverage call when the
pending-source index is empty. These host-side changes preserve execution and
accounting semantics and are included in the measured result.

Round-1 gate against frozen controls: all **80** existing path/workload cells
passed **+0.5%** (largest +0.485%, I286 `smc-rmw-64`); store widths +0.092% (8)
and +0.051% (32). These tables are superseded by the round-3 gate below.
No commits, git writes, bead operations or pushes were performed.


**READ quirk inventory / before and after:** C1 exposed neither catalogue
entry; C2 supplies the following model-specific behavior. Existing alias and
system-model behavior is retained. UM means `oracles/intel/i8086-um.pdf`;
PRM means `oracles/intel/i286-prm.pdf`. Each named witness is in
[legacy_i8086.rs](../../x86jit-tests/tests/legacy/legacy_i8086.rs) and runs both models.
The page images for these derivations were checked in round 1.

| Quirk or derived item | C2 value / source | Witness |
|---|---|---|
| PUSH SP | Store decremented SP; SDM Vol 3B §25.17.1; PRM App. C item 8 p. C-2 | `push_sp_stores_decremented_value` |
| Stored FLAGS 15:12 | Fh, including interrupt/exception frames; SDM Vol 3B §25.17.2 | `stored_flags_are_ones_and_popf_cannot_change_them`, `divide_error_saves_next_ip_and_signed_minimum_is_outside_range`, `external_interrupt_precedes_single_step_and_handler_is_steppable` |
| Shift/rotate count | Full CL, through 255; UM §2.7 p. 2-39 | `shifts_and_rotates_use_all_eight_count_bits` |
| Divide return IP | Next instruction; UM §2.6 p. 2-26 | `divide_error_saves_next_ip_and_signed_minimum_is_outside_range`, `divide_outward_metadata_names_next_ip` |
| Signed minimum quotient | 80h/8000h raises interrupt 0; UM §2.7 p. 2-37 | `divide_error_saves_next_ip_and_signed_minimum_is_outside_range` |
| Length limit | None; PRM App. C item 10 p. C-2 | `prefix_runs_longer_than_fifteen_and_255_have_no_length_fault`, `a_full_ip_wrap_inside_one_instruction_ends_the_shared_block` |
| Operand offset | Each byte wraps within the segment; UM §2.3 p. 2-12 | `word_and_far_pointer_bytes_wrap_inside_segment`, `stack_word_wraps_and_ip_wraps_at_ffff` |
| Sequential IP | Wrap to zero; UM §§2.2–2.3 pp. 2-7–12 | `nop_and_halt_at_ffff_wrap_the_sequential_ip` |
| Formed address | Truncate at twenty bits, no A20 gate; UM §2.8 p. 2-68 | `no_system_state_is_consulted_and_a20_cannot_change_the_bus`, `fetch_wraps_twenty_bits_and_smc_refetches_the_following_instruction` |
| Processor interrupts | Only 0–4; UM §2.6 pp. 2-24–28 | `catalogue_and_bus_siblings`, `empty_socket_and_emulation_are_per_vcpu_engine_policies` |
| Undefined encodings | Typed refusal, including POP CS and MOV CS; UM §4.2 pp. 4-27–35; PRM App. C item 6 p. C-2; accepted aliases as recorded above | `later_and_undefined_encodings_are_typed_refusals`, `compatibility_aliases_follow_the_accepted_model_policy` |
| LOCK | Typed refusal: UM §2.5 pp. 2-17–18 requires whole-instruction bus ownership, including REP | `later_and_undefined_encodings_are_typed_refusals` |
| External IRQ and TF | Enter external handler before single-step trap; UM §2.6 pp. 2-26–28 | `external_interrupt_precedes_single_step_and_handler_is_steppable` |
| Interrupted multi-prefix REP | Save IP of last prefix, retaining completed elements; UM §2.7 p. 2-42 | `interrupted_rep_resumes_at_last_prefix_in_both_orders`, `resumed_multi_prefix_string_loses_the_other_prefix` |
| Undefined flags | Deterministic engine values, fully listed in spec §17.10; guests must not rely on them; UM §2.7 pp. 2-35–40 | `undefined_status_flags_have_a_deterministic_engine_value` |
| AAM/AAD second byte | Manual prints 0Ah; other immediates refused; UM §4.2 Table 4-12 p. 4-24 | `aam_aad_documented_second_byte_and_undefined_flag_policy` |

## C2 I8088 and I8086, round 2 (baseline d4a58e2a)

**MEASURED result:** all **80** existing path/workload cells pass **+0.5%**
against fresh paired controls of the same saved d4a58e2a executable. Largest
increase: **+0.410%**, `alu` / `legacy-i286-a20-off`.
Both store widths pass (-0.027% / -0.049%). These round-2 tables are
superseded by the round-3 gate below.

**MEASURED review witnesses / READ fixes:** six adopted adversarial tests failed
against the saved round-1 library on both models. Their transfers and assertions
are retained in `legacy_i8086`, extended to run both catalogue entries.

| Finding | Witness in `legacy_i8086` | Fix | Before → final |
|---|---|---|---|
| Word-string MMIO retry | `wrapped_scalar_and_string_mmio_roundtrips`, `string_write_reports_second_mmio_byte` | Replace the current fault-byte marker on each fault; acknowledge actual byte values | Repeated 4000h → 4000h then 4001h, once each; pass |
| IRET delay | `iret_defers_irq_through_following_instruction` | Lift a flag restore that arms inhibition through its successor; POPF does not | IRQ before INC → INC before IRQ; pass |
| Present FPU MMIO | `present_fpu_memory_operand_mmio_is_resumable`, `coprocessor_mmio_read_and_write_complete_once_per_bus_byte`, `coprocessor_large_image_mmio_fits_the_fixed_retry_ledger` | Preserve the complete memory error and distinguish CPU dummy reads from coprocessor transfers; pack large images within the existing ledger | UnmappedMemory → answerable byte MMIO and complete read/write/94-byte-image retries; pass |
| WrappedString evidence | `wrapped_string_fault_evidence_names_bus_transfers`, `completed_rep_iterations_are_in_fault_progress` | Add the string descriptor/address resolution; map ledger entries to bus addresses and conservative REP/RAM progress | Internal keys and false entry progress → real transfers and restart evidence; pass |
| 83h /1,/4,/6 amendment | `logical_83_forms_sign_extend_the_immediate`, `compatibility_amendment_keeps_other_encodings_absent` | Admit sign-extended OR/AND/XOR register and memory forms; retain all listed refusals | Refusal → defined operation; all refusal witnesses pass |

**READ primary sources:** IRET inhibition is specified by
`oracles/intel/i8086-um.pdf` §2.6 p. 2-24. The ordinary 83h forms are in
`oracles/intel/sdm.pdf`, Vol 2A §3.3 AND p. 3-60, Vol 2B §4.3 OR p. 4-163,
Vol 2D §6.1 XOR p. 6-40. Every cited value was checked against its page image.
`oracles/ss8088/v2/metadata.json` marks these forms "normal"; it corroborates
the maintainer's primary-source amendment. The corpus does not license
semantics, and the C3 differential lane was not run. The I80286 TEST /1 witness
in `legacy_model_decode` cites the accepted amendment and retains its existing
behavior. 60h–6Fh, C0h/C1h, C8h/C9h, FFh /7, D6h and D0–D3 /6 remain refused.

**READ API and integration:** the catalogue requires the two CpuModel variants,
ModelIsa::I8086 and the 8086 quirk fields (§2). LegacyOp was already a public
enum with public variants; its eight C2 variants retain that pattern. Rust does
not permit private visibility for individual enum variants. No CpuState or
jit_abi layout changes were made. LegacyOp remains 56 bytes and IrOp 72 bytes,
as in C1. Round 2 makes no memory.rs edits; round 1's sole hunk uses wrapping
arithmetic when RunningMemory derives the next instruction's physical address.
In interp/mod.rs, round 2 adds cold bus-evidence qualification and the lifted
IRET delay hook to round 1's Direct20 dispatch/wrapped-operation integration.
These hunks are separate from ordinary adjacent-region access handling.

**READ / pairing policy:** `None` performs the CPU ESC dummy word read and
discards it (UM §2.7 p. 2-48; iAPX 86/88,186/188 PRM §6.3 p. 6-8).
`Present` retains the generic x87 engine with resumable operand transfers,
without an 8087-fidelity claim. C6 supplies the generation-faithful pairing.
**ASSUMED engine policy:** WAIT with `None` uses ready TEST.
The original quirk/derived-item inventory and citations are above; spec §17.10
lists the deterministic undefined-flag values, on which a guest must not rely.
I8086/I8088 instruction results are identical in UM Appendix B pp. B-31–32;
bus width, prefetch and timing differences have no cycle-table implementation yet.

(21), 421 core tests, 37 C2 tests covering both models, boundary/environment,

Application/alias IR images match C1 byte for byte; the existing Application

The final isolated fences run passed six active tests with two threads in

**MEASURED SMC attribution:** complete campaigns 1–7 missed 6/4/6/5/8/5/5
SMC cells. Returning DecodeSet rows by value, the system-only reference decoder
shortcut, and the shared register-MOV qualification shortcut did not remove the
cost and were reverted. The last shortcut missed the steady-state path: warmed
SMC uses retained one-instruction IR and re-fetches its bytes on every entry.
Next-instruction SMC reuses that IR after its constant store, while RMW can also
rebuild the changed MOV. Cache empty/lazy/coverage/hash-builder guards remain
covered by the cache witnesses; the decoder-flags projection showed a small
next-instruction benefit and no consistent RMW benefit. Those changes left the measured SMC misses.

**MEASURED component localization:** varying a retained Checked MOV from 2 to

showed excess slopes of 9.208, 5.622, 9.977 host instructions/byte on I80286;
6.326, 4.154, 5.222 on I80386. The generated sixth code selected a profile,
loaded its ISA, selected a decode-set pointer and loaded its prefix-policy bit
on every byte. C1 used a generation check. The final single change projects
exact existing catalogue-row constants by generation, retaining prefix policy
without those pointer selections. No policy value, identity digest, or public
API changes in that host-cost change.

reference sample. The removed pointer loads are also absent in generated code.

## C2 I8088 and I8086, round 3 (baseline d4a58e2a)

**MEASURED result:** all **80** existing path/workload cells pass **+0.5%**
against fresh paired controls of the saved d4a58e2a executable, interleaved
in one campaign. Largest increase: **+0.249%**, `smc-rmw-64` / I286 A20 on.
Store widths: -0.003% (8) and -0.006% (32).

**MEASURED review witnesses / READ fixes:** a read-only adversarial review of
round 2 reported one P2 and five P3 findings. Each witness below failed on the
round-2 code and passes after its fix. The reviewer's sequences are adopted in
`legacy_i8086` unchanged in substance and run on both models.

| Finding | Witness | Fix | Before → final |
|---|---|---|---|
| P2-1 REP owner at a yield | `budget_yield_with_pending_irq_keeps_the_prefix_owner`, `budget_yield_with_pending_irq_rep_first_order`; control `budget_yield_with_pending_irq_then_delivery_names_the_last_prefix` | The walk never moves IP to the last prefix. The dispatcher keeps the owner/restart pair and applies it at delivery (UM §2.7 p. 2-42) | Yield IP 1202h; bytes 11 BB CC, or CX 2 → 1201h; 11 22 33, CX 0 |
| P3-1 RAM fault opened a retry | `unmapped_string_fault_does_not_open_an_mmio_retry` | The fault marker records whether it opened the retry; a RAM-side fault closes that retry again | `irq_ready` false, string first → as I80286: ready, IRQ first |
| P3-2 IRET delay with IF set | `sti_and_iret_delay_only_when_they_reenable_interrupts` | IRET arms the delay only for IF 0 → 1, as STI does. UM §2.6 p. 2-24 (page image checked) says both "reenable interrupts only after the end of the following instruction" | AX 1 → 0 |
| P3-3 I8088 read I8086 rows | `lift::i8086::tests::lowering_reads_the_supplied_catalogue_entry` | The lift buffer carries the selected entry into shared lowering; delivery and resolve read `cpu_model`. The generation projection returns to its C1 values | Changed row ignored → followed |
| P3-4 deprecated selector ran I8086 | `deprecated_selector_keeps_refusing_the_8086_generation` | The Direct20 entry refuses a VM without an 8088/8086 catalogue model through the C1 cold entry | `BudgetExhausted` and execution → `LegacyEntryFacility`, state unchanged |
| P3-5 decode rows | `model::decode::i8086::tests::every_8086_row_is_admitted_by_some_encoding` | The 8086 bit is cleared on every row the 8086 policy never admits: ARPL, SALC, D0–D3 /6, the 66h/REX NOP widths and the ten combined WAIT forms (their C1 values) | 18 rows → none |

Two consequences of these fixes also have witnesses. Before P3-2, the
unconditional IRET delay was the only thing that refreshed the Direct20
boundary after a flag restore loaded TF. POPF never had that refresh, so in
round 2 a POPF that loaded TF with IF clear was never single-stepped.
`flag_restores_loading_tf_trap_after_the_following_instruction` covers POPF and
IRET (HLT reached, BX 1 → trap after INC AX; UM §2.6 p. 2-28). The fast path now
refreshes its boundary when TF is set after a Direct20 block. After P3-1 an IRQ
can be delivered directly after a RAM-side fault on a string's first element,
and the recorded pair would have named the last prefix although no element had
completed. `irq_after_a_string_fault_names_the_last_prefix_only_after_an_element`
shows frame 1201h → 1200h. The pair is now kept only after a completed element.

**MEASURED gate attribution:** the first complete campaign missed 19 cells
(`alu`, `load`, `load-odd`, `call-ret` on every system path, plus one `store`
and two `ip-boundary-64` cells; worst +0.880%, `load` / I386). Interleaved runs
of d4a58e2a, the round-2 final executable and round-3 builds placed the cost in
round 3, not in host drift: `alu` +0.27–0.31% for round 2 against +0.63–0.81%
for round 3, and `call-ret` +0.01–0.07% against +0.93–0.96%. The system walk
instantiations were byte-identical to round 2. `Vcpu::run_steps`, which inlines
the 110 KB system dispatcher, was not. Its run-entry test had been changed to
read `cpu_model` for P3-4. A separate lift buffer type for P3-3 also added
about 70 KB of lifter monomorphizations; replacing it with a field on the
existing buffer did not move those cells. Restoring the round-2 generation test
at the run entries, with the deprecated refusal inside the out-of-line Direct20
entry, brought `alu` to -0.04%…-0.02% and `load` to -0.01%…+0.04%. All samples
of the failed campaign and of the triads are retained with the round's
scratch evidence.

## Accesses across adjacent regions (xj-ir6.12) — known regression

**Accepted by the maintainer for landing (2026-11-14):** a Real16 embedder needs the
split now. The +0.5 % gate is not met and is not relaxed; bead xj-ir6.31 carries the
miss (decision-23 amendment).

### Change — READ

A data access that misses region containment but whose bytes all lie in adjacent
RAM/ROM-kind regions is performed instead of ending with `UnmappedMemory` (spec.md,
"Data accesses across adjacent regions"). Each containment miss in `memory.rs`
(scalar read, write and write probe; wide checked read and write) tail-calls an
out-of-line, non-generic function behind a small observer wrapper. Gaps, Trap bytes
and LOCK operands keep the previous exit.

### Measurement method — MEASURED

Base: d4a58e2a. Candidate: that commit with the change in `memory.rs` only (sha256
`468add41c8add4c2257d231290f4351097b387c689298d9e3932f637ee66b256`). Both built
with the release profile as committed (no codegen-units or other profile change),
`CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=4`, same toolchain, same host (Apple M5 Pro, macOS, aarch64).
`real16_loops 1 3 <workload>` for every workload and all five paths: host
instructions per guest step, the median of three serial rounds. Store probe: a copy
of `interpreter_store_probe` that reads `proc_pid_rusage` instructions around the
measured run, 200000 iterations × 16 stores, three processes per side. The base
campaign ran before the candidate's, not interleaved.

### Host instructions per guest step — MEASURED

|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
