### Attribution and follow-up — MEASURED / ASSUMED

MEASURED: workloads with no guest data access regress too (`alu` +1.2 % on the
Machine paths). Between base and candidate, 2088 single-home items change codegen
unit (`-Zprint-mono-items`, diagnostic build only), and functions in untouched
modules change size: for example `TranslationCache::get_for_dispatch` grows from
824 to 1556 bytes because `DispatchMap::get_accounted` is now inlined into it.
MEASURED with x86jit-core at one codegen unit on both sides (diagnostic only),
`real16_loops` stays within +0.37 %, but the store probe still costs +1.05 % at
width 8 and +8.25 % at width 32. A successful split rejoins the hot code after its
cold call. `vstore::<false, 0, Memory>` grows from 3148 to 3844 bytes and saves 8
callee-saved registers instead of 2.

Result (xj-ir6.31): the split now runs off the hot path. The transfer functions are
containment-only again, and a containment miss over adjacent regions re-executes
the rest of the block through a cold path. The gate base is a373f11b (main after
C4a) with 060d446a reverted, code only. In the final campaign on 176c4681, every
non-SMC cell of `real16_loops` and every store-probe cell is within +0.5 %
(MEASURED). Over 15 processes per side, `smc-next-64` misses on the four
twenty-bit paths (+0.500 % to +0.529 %); see "Split off the hot path
(xj-ir6.31)" below. The first round
measured against f15060b9 (0 of 80 cells over, worst +0.241 %) and is kept there
as history, as are the tables above.

### Review fixes on top of the measured candidate — MEASURED

The tree that lands also carries three review fixes outside `memory.rs`: the first
8-byte transfer of a 16-byte `vstore` is stored only when one region contains it,
and a containment miss checks both transfers before either is written; a LOCK
operand split by the A20 gate keeps the containment miss for each bus piece; an
automatic child view joins adjacent regions of one kind for data accesses. Quick
interleaved A/B against the measured candidate binaries above (same method):
`real16_loops` `alu`, `load`, `store`, `push-pop` and `ip-boundary-1` on all five
paths change by −0.09 % to +0.23 %. The store probe at width 32 and at width 8
without recovery changes by less than ±0.1 %. At width 8 with recovery the single
case is +0.74 % and the crossing case −3.04 %. The cause is an inlining change in
`usercopy.rs`, which the fixes do not touch. `usercopy::copy_out` now has 26 call
sites instead of 49 and is inlined into `usercopy::store`. The crossing path saves
a call, and every recovery-mode scalar store saves one more register pair.

## C4a NecV20 and NecV30 (baseline 060d446a)

**MEASURED result:** 106 of the 112 path/workload cells are within **+0.5%** of
060d446a. Each cell is the median of three interleaved process pairs in one
campaign. **Six cells miss the gate, all on SMC workloads:**
- `smc-next-1` on I8088 and I8086: +1.35% and +1.33%;
- `smc-next-64` on the alias, I8088 and I8086: +0.50%, +0.81% and +0.79%;
- `smc-rmw-64` on the alias: +0.55%.

On I8088/I8086, comparing processes in the same mode (see the variance
bullet below), all three SMC workloads are +0.70% to +0.91%. `ip-boundary-1`
passes on every path, at −0.92% to −1.36%. The four system paths pass every
cell. The store probe is +0.018% (8) and +0.010% (32). **The gate is not met.**
What remains follows a codegen-unit partition change, and the partition change
comes from the public `Exit::I8080Mode` variant itself (mechanism below). This
round stops at its bounded effort; the miss is reported for the supervisor.

**Change in this round (READ):** one attribute. `lift::i8086::nec_instruction`, the
NEC V20/V30 lowering, is `#[inline(never)]`. Before, it was inlined into the
shared 8086 lowering `lift::i8086::instruction`, which every twenty-bit lift runs:
that function grew from 8,984 to 17,280 bytes. With the attribute it is
8,804 bytes, and `nec_instruction` is a separate 8,452-byte function (MEASURED,
`nm` sizes of `real16_loops`). Nothing else changed.

**MEASURED reproduction:** the delivered tree (4614dfa3), measured once
against 060d446a in one interleaved campaign. The `ip-boundary-1` miss no
longer appears: −0.93% to −1.38% on all seven paths. The miss is now in
8 cells, all on SMC workloads on the alias and I8088/I8086 paths:
- `smc-next-1` I8088 and I8086: +0.65% and +0.67%;
- `smc-next-64` on the alias, I8088 and I8086: +0.83%, +1.55% and +1.61%;
- `smc-rmw-64` on the alias, I8088 and I8086: +0.55%, +0.92% and +0.90%.

The four system paths pass every cell, at −2.1% to +0.17%. The store probe
changes by +0.001% (8) and −0.000% (32).

**MEASURED mechanism:**
- *`ip-boundary-1` is a layout effect, not NEC code.* 060d446a already
  has the shape the f15060b9 campaign blamed on C4a. In `Vcpu::run_steps` and
  `run_direct20`, `RunGuard::finish` is inlined, while
  `LocalKey::with::<RunGuard::finish closure>` and the `time_stats::LOCAL`
  accessor shim are called out of line, three times in `run_steps` (objdump
  call lists). The C4a tree has the f15060b9 shape: `RunGuard::finish` is one
  call, and the TLS access is inlined inside it. The shape follows rustc's
  codegen-unit partition of x86jit-core. In 060d446a the `LocalKey::with`
  closures of `RunGuard::{enter, finish}`, `RunGuard::drop` and
  `dispatch_counts` are in a different CGU from `RunGuard` itself. In C4a they
  are in the same CGU (see the partition bullet below).
- *Partition (diagnostic `-Zprint-mono-items`, x86jit-core only; profile
  unchanged).* In 060d446a, `memory::Memory` shares a CGU with the
  `std::collections::BTreeMap` generic instances. In C4a those instances move
  to the CGU of the B-tree node code, and the `std::thread::LocalKey`
  instances move in with `memory::Memory`. The `RwLockWriteGuard` drops of the
  shared dispatch map move from another CGU into the one that holds
  `cache::TranslationCache`. The result in `real16_loops`:
  - `Memory::refresh_code_aliases` shrinks from 3,368 to 1,544 bytes, with
    B-tree entry inserts out of line;
  - two `TranslationCache::insert_checked` instances (the closures of
    `vm::finish_single` and `vm::legacy::resolve_direct_uncached`) shrink from
    4,488 and 4,544 bytes to 1,648 and 1,712, with `DispatchMapWrite` and
    `RwLockWriteGuard` drop glue out of line;
  - `invalidate_range` for SMC shrinks from 5,172 to 3,704 bytes.

  These run on every SMC re-lift through the shared cache, which the alias
  and I8088/I8086 use and the I286/I386 private interpreter cache does not
  (READ, `Vcpu::run_steps` docs). C4a does not change these functions
  (READ, diff).
- *What moves the partition* (diagnostic, 060d446a's core with parts of C4a
  added):
  - the six new `UnsupportedSystemOperation` variants alone: 060d446a partition;
  - the new public types (`I8080View`, `I8080Entry`, `I8080Handback`,
    `I8080HandbackError`) alone, with no new `Exit` variant: 060d446a
    partition;
  - those types plus the `Exit::I8080Mode` variant: the C4a partition, with
    either the folded `from_exit` arm or a panicking one.

  The public variant itself moves rustc's CGU merge. Neither the
  `#[inline(never)]` nor a trial that moved the BRKEM scan out of the
  dispatcher changes the partition. That trial removed 69 `find_map`
  instances; it was not measured and is reverted.
- *SMC cells vary between processes.* Within one process, the three rounds of
  an SMC cell differ by up to 0.48%. Between processes, the I8088/I8086 SMC
  cells take one of two values 0.55% to 0.70% apart, on both sides. For
  example, `smc-next-1` on I8088 over seven processes per side: 060d446a gives
  21,326 or 21,462, and this round gives 21,483–21,494 or 21,615–21,632. The
  alias SMC cells vary by up to 0.3%. ASSUMED cause: per-process hash seeds
  (`RandomState` maps in `cache.rs`) and heap addresses. A median of three
  can therefore land in either mode; comparing like modes gives the
  +0.70% to +0.91% above (seven processes per side).
- *The attribute's effect* (head to head, delivered tree against this round,
  three process pairs): `smc-next-64` −0.65% (I8088), −0.64% (I8086) and
  −0.26% (alias); `smc-next-1` and the system paths within ±0.03%.

**MEASURED method:** dev-shell Rust 1.98.1, macOS arm64 (Apple M5 Pro),
background QoS, four build jobs, release profile as committed. Baseline:
`git archive 060d446a`, built in its own target (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`aec0d26a7c05565f351d82ae1e60df0ad655ea4f7de8ecd973e4de741e646787`). Candidate:
the worktree (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`ebdf2e4c76f37f222116922b1d9553eccc6c2929a0ca00f65d1351dbb297fedc`). For every
workload, literal `real16_loops 1 3 <workload>`, baseline then candidate, one
process at a time, three such pairs per workload in one campaign. A cell is
the median over processes of each process's three-round median of host
instructions per step. Equal steps and retirements are asserted. The store
probe is the counted `interpreter_store_probe` driver, built against each core
library, with three interleaved pairs per width. Another agent's builds and
benchmarks ran on the host, as did this round's own checks. Only instruction
counts are used.

**MEASURED gate:** host instructions per step, 060d446a → this round; median over three process pairs.

### Review fixes on top of this round — MEASURED quick check

The C4a review fixes (xj-50w.7) change the NEC refusals, the NEC EI lowering
(out of line, in `nec_instruction`), the hand-back checks and the dispatcher's
post-block NEC test, which loses its single-step branch. Quick check against
this round's binary, same method: three interleaved process pairs of literal
`real16_loops 1 3 <workload>`; fixes `real16_loops` SHA-256
`d71f49b2d4873faa9a2f9bd33dca476d84f6a75fbbe1d019f54ffb8384416873`.

access inlined), and the diagnostic partition keeps every module grouping.

### Round 1 against f15060b9 (history)

**MEASURED result:** 101 of the 112 existing path/workload cells are within
**+0.5%** of f15060b9, measured interleaved in one campaign. **Eleven cells miss
the gate:** `ip-boundary-1` on all seven paths (+1.13% to +1.38%) and
`smc-next-1` on the four system paths (+0.56% to +0.75%). Both workloads run
one guest step per `run_steps` call; the excess is about 27-40 host
instructions per call on paths that execute no NEC code (the alias, I286, I386),
so it is per-call code, not NEC lowering. Store probe: +0.143% (8), +0.092% (32),
within the gate. The miss is reported for the supervisor; it is not traded away.

**MEASURED attribution (not resolved):**
- The only edit to code every walk shares is the exhaustive conversion
  `InterpExit::from_exit`, which needs an arm for `Exit::I8080Mode`. With a
  panicking `unreachable!` arm, every `interpret_block_inner` instantiation grew
  by about 800 bytes and the I8088/I8086 walks ran +0.47% to +1.14% host
  instructions per step (`alu` 402.264→405.523, `push-pop` 651.264→658.701 on
  I8088), while `ip-boundary-1` stayed within -0.07% to +0.12% on every path.
- With the arm folded into `Interrupted` (the change as delivered) the per-step
  cells pass, but `Vcpu::run_steps` changes shape (objdump): seven inline
  thread-local accesses become four, three `time_stats::LOCAL` accesses go
  through the key's accessor shim (`FnOnce::call_once`), and
  `RunGuard::finish` is inlined with its `LocalKey::with` closure out of line.
  The same shape appears in `run_direct20`. That is the per-call excess.
- Variants built and measured on `ip-boundary-1`: an `unreachable_unchecked`
  arm (walks within 12 bytes of f15060b9; shape unchanged; +1.26% to +1.48%), a
  cold diverging function (walks +500 bytes; shape unchanged),
  `#[inline(never)]` on `RunGuard::finish` and `dispatch_counts` (+1.78% to
  +2.04%), on `RunGuard::finish` alone (+1.74% to +1.91%), and the NEC vCPU
  methods moved into `vm/i8086.rs` instead of a new module (+1.25% to +1.39%).
  None meets both gates. Which inlining or codegen-unit decision moves the
  accessor is **ASSUMED** to be a ThinLTO import effect outside the NEC code; it
  was not isolated.

**MEASURED method:** dev-shell Rust 1.98.1, macOS arm64, background QoS, four
build jobs. Baseline: `git archive f15060b9`, built in its own target
(`real16_loops` SHA-256
`5b5e8e1fdc56bda60714752bc31d1ef744b9081fe1c8caba5881b7fdf491d15b`). Candidate:
the delivered source (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`4f40c18298fdc1f0cbec06ef390d35066ad557e6158f37ee52691f7a06ebb5e9`; a rebuild of
the delivered tree reproduces it byte for byte). Each
workload runs literal `real16_loops 1 3 <workload>` for the baseline, then the
candidate, one process at a time, all sixteen workloads in one campaign; cells
are three-round medians of host instructions per step, with equal steps,
retirements and warmup asserted. Other builds and tests ran on the host; only
instruction counts are used. The store probe is the C2 counted
`interpreter_store_probe` driver built against each core library, three
interleaved pairs per width. Catalogue identities of I8088, I8086, I80286 and
I80386 are equal in both builds (`22fb677cf449d2fe`, `22fb677cf449d2f0`,
`3699e02a8ef13e63`, `e8d00c17f97eb71f`), and `size_of` `Exit`, `IrOp`,
`StepRun` and `CpuState` are 32, 72, 40 and 3808 in both.

## Split off the hot path (xj-ir6.31)

Gate base: a373f11b (main after C4a) with 060d446a reverted, code only — main
without the split. The first round measured against f15060b9, before C4a landed;
it is kept below as history.

### Change — READ

- The scalar and wide transfers in `memory.rs` are containment-only, with the
  bodies they had before xj-ir6.12. The walk's providers (`RunningMemory`,
  `ChildMemory`) return the containment miss unchanged. An associated constant,
  `InterpreterMemory::SPLIT`, is false for them, so their `vstore` compiles no split
  branch.
- A data access over adjacent RAM/ROM regions first ends with the usual
  `UnmappedMemory` exit, with no register of that instruction committed. The code that
  already handles that exit calls a cold, non-inlined function: the application and
  alias dispatchers' exit arms (`vm/legacy.rs`), `execute_shared` on the I286 and
  I386 Machine path (`interp/legacy.rs`), and `interpret_block_mode` for `step_one`
  and `interpret_block` (`interp/mod.rs`).
- That function walks the rest of the block again, from the faulting instruction,
  through the existing retry walk. The retry walk's provider, `SplitMemory`, performs
  the span with the out-of-line functions that xj-ir6.12 added. Re-execution is
  restart-safe because a fault exit leaves the instruction's registers uncommitted
  (spec.md §16; SDM Vol 3A §§7.5-7.6), and stores made before the miss are made
  again with the same values. The real-mode stack-frame helpers
  (`interp/control.rs`: interrupt delivery, far CALL/RET, IRET, PUSHA/POPA, ENTER,
  PUSHF/POPF) commit SP word by word, so they perform the span themselves
  (`read_across_regions` / `write_across_regions`), exactly as the walk did before
  this change. The rest of the block ends where the block ends, so retirement, step
  and block accounting equal one walk of the block.
- If the re-execution stops at the same access without completing an instruction,
  with a guest-map containment miss of its own (a gap, a Trap byte or a LOCK
  operand), the first exit is returned with its evidence. Any other exit there, such
  as a host fault at the operand's first byte, is the re-execution's.
- Out-of-walk callers use `Memory`: JIT fallback helpers, Machine bus helpers, x87
  memory operands and user copy. Its public accessors try containment first and call
  the cold span function only on a miss. A 16-byte `vstore` through `Memory` or
  `SplitMemory` goes to the out-of-line `vstore16_split`, which keeps the review fix:
  both transfers are checked before either is written.
- A one-byte access lies in one region or in none, so the scalar `*_spanning`
  entry points call the span functions only for a wider access. The twenty-bit
  walk (I8088, I8086, V20, V30) moves every data byte through a one-byte transfer
  (`interp/i8086.rs`; `lift/i8086.rs` lowers loads, stores and strings to its
  Wrapped primitives), so no containment miss over adjacent regions reaches it:
  `execute_shared20` keeps its shape without the re-execution, and its byte helpers
  keep their containment-only shape.

### Codegen pins — MEASURED / READ

The change moves code between codegen units, and some unrelated inlining decisions
flip. Each pin below keeps the shape that the commit before xj-ir6.12 had. The
numbers come from intermediate candidates of the first round. After C4a, the
shapes were checked again in the rebased `real16_loops` and store-probe binaries
(READ, objdump call lists): `usercopy::store` calls `copy_out` once on both sides;
`Execution::sync_class` makes no `eq` call on either side; `RunGuard::finish` and
`finish_with_counts` are single calls with the thread-local access inline. The
gate base instead inlines `RunGuard::finish` into `Vcpu::run_steps` with three
out-of-line `LocalKey::with` calls and three accessor-shim calls. That matches
`ip-boundary-1` running −1.5 % to −2.0 % below the base on every path (ASSUMED
attribution). No pin was redundant or harmful in that check, so all are kept.

- `usercopy::copy_out` is `#[inline(never)]`. Without it, it is inlined into
  `usercopy::store`, and the store probe at width 8, single, recovery on costs
  +0.67 % (MEASURED).
- `time_stats::RunGuard::finish` is `#[inline(never)]` and uses
  `LocalKey::try_with`. Without that, it is inlined with an out-of-line
  `LocalKey::with` call (READ).
- The run's dispatch counts are added in the same thread-local access as the exit
  count (`DispatchGuard::finish` calls `RunGuard::finish_with_counts`). Without that,
  the dispatchers reach the thread-local through an out-of-line accessor call (READ),
  and `ip-boundary-1` is +0.56 % to +0.75 % on the gated paths instead of −0.27 % to
  −0.33 % (MEASURED; that is the only difference between the two candidates).
- `LegacyContextStamp` has a hand-written `PartialEq` marked `#[inline(always)]`.
  Without it, the derived `eq` is an out-of-line call in `sync_class` (READ).
- The alias dispatcher calls its split only from an `Exit::UnmappedMemory` arm. A
  call on every exit cost `ip-boundary-1` +1.9 % on the Alias path (MEASURED).

### Final campaign on 176c4681 — MEASURED

The tree reviewed as 176c4681 (the rebase round plus the review fixes below),
against the gate base. The candidate binaries are byte-identical to a build of
`git archive 176c4681` (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`2f67d45864b939b6ea9e3467be07ee80909293180e5b4008c2d9f382abe036e4`). Method as in
the rebase round. All 144 cells are measured in one interleaved campaign. The SMC
cells also have 15 interleaved processes per side, for each of the three SMC
workloads.

**The gate is not met in 4 of 144 cells: `smc-next-64` on I8088, I8086, V20 and
V30, at +0.520 %, +0.529 %, +0.500 % and +0.517 % (median of 15 processes).**
- The 117 non-SMC cells are within +0.5 %, worst +0.360 % (`call-ret`, Alias).
  The twenty-bit paths are at −1.99 % to +0.030 %.
- The SMC cells on the Alias and I286/I386 paths pass, at −0.77 % to +0.16 %.
- On the twenty-bit paths, `smc-next-1` is +0.27 % to +0.28 % and `smc-rmw-64`
  +0.47 % to +0.48 % (both pass).
- Every store-probe cell is below the base (worst −0.066 %).

On the twenty-bit SMC cells the base still takes one of two values per process,
about 0.55 % apart. The candidate now takes a single value in all 15 processes:
- `smc-next-64` and `smc-rmw-64`: 0.04 % to 0.09 % below the base's upper value.
- `smc-next-1`: between the two values, 0.27 % above the lower one and 0.25 %
  below the upper one.

The median therefore compares mostly against the base's more frequent lower value.
What selects the value is not known (ASSUMED, as in C4a: per-process layout).

### Rebase round against a373f11b without 060d446a — result (MEASURED, superseded by the final campaign)

**The gate is not met in 4 of 144 cells.** Every non-SMC cell is within +0.5 %:
117 cells, worst +0.339 % (`call-ret`, Alias); the twenty-bit paths are at
−1.97 % to +0.064 %. Every store-probe cell is below the base (worst −0.077 %).
The SMC cells on the Alias and the I286/I386 paths pass (−0.80 % to +0.17 %). On
the twenty-bit paths each process lands in one of two values about 0.6 % apart, as
the C4a round found, so these cells were measured over repeated processes:
`smc-next-1` and `smc-rmw-64` pass on the median of processes (at most +0.478 %);
`smc-next-64` misses on I8088, I8086, V20 and V30 at +0.657 % to +0.683 %. Within
one mode it changes by +0.005 % to +0.206 %. The miss is in how often a process
takes the upper value: 13 of 15 candidate processes against 3 of 15 base
processes. What selects the value is not known (ASSUMED, as in C4a: per-process
hash seeds and heap addresses). The two designs this round allows were spent on
the twenty-bit memory cells below, so this is the best state.

### Rebase round: what the rebased tree needed — MEASURED / READ

- The rebased tree as handed over (WIP 9b3c4f15) missed 26 of 144 cells, all on
  the twenty-bit paths, in one interleaved campaign: `load` and `load-odd`
  +1.20 % to +1.23 %, `call-ret` +1.00 %, `push-pop` +0.92 %, `dos-mixed` +0.70 %,
  `straight-line` +0.68 % (I8088, I8086), `store` +0.54 % to +0.56 % (MEASURED).
  Two causes (READ, objdump): `interp::i8086::read` and `write` saved 10
  callee-saved registers instead of 2, because their byte loops kept values live
  across the cold span call; `execute_shared20` grew from 708 to 912 bytes and
  saved 12 registers instead of 4, to keep its arguments for the re-execution.
- Design 1, the one-byte guard in the scalar `*_spanning` entry points: the
  twenty-bit byte helpers have the base's code again (READ). The twenty-bit memory
  cells fell to +0.20 % to +0.48 % (MEASURED).
- Design 2, no re-execution on the twenty-bit walk: `execute_shared20` has the
  base's code except one branch condition (READ). The twenty-bit non-SMC cells are
  now at −1.97 % to +0.064 % (MEASURED, table below).
- C4a changed `execute_shared` so that a twenty-bit `ModeChange` leaves the
  boundary to the dispatcher. `split_shared` follows the rule for the other walks,
  and with design 2 it never runs on the twenty-bit walk (READ).

### Rebase round: measurement method — MEASURED

- Base: a373f11b with 060d446a reverted, code only, as handed over (uncommitted).
  Candidate: the rebased tree with designs 1 and 2. Each is built from its own
  source snapshot into its own target directory, with the release profile as
  committed, `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=4`, the same toolchain and the same host (Apple M5
  Pro, macOS, aarch64). The final tree produces the same `__text` bytes as the
  measured candidate.
- `real16_loops 1 3 <workload>` for all 16 workloads and all nine paths it has
  (Alias, I8088, I8086, V20, V30, I286, I386, and I286/I386 with A20 off): 144
  cells, each the median of three rounds in one process. Base and candidate
  alternate for each workload, and the order rotates between workloads.
- SMC cells: extra interleaved processes per side (15 for `smc-next-64`, 11 for
  `smc-next-1`, 5 for `smc-rmw-64`). A cell is the median over processes; the mode
  columns split the values at the largest gap (twenty-bit paths only).
- Store probe: as above, three rounds of all eight cells, in rotating side order.
- One benchmark process at a time; builds and test runs shared the host.

### Review fixes after the rebase round — MEASURED / READ

A review of the rebase-round tree found two regressions against 060d446a. Both
are fixed; behaviour equals 060d446a again (MEASURED).

- Real-mode interrupt delivery in the walk committed SP after each frame word. A
  CS or IP word over two adjacent RAM regions missed after FLAGS was pushed, and
  the re-execution pushed a second frame lower (Alias, INT n and INT3). The frame
  helpers now perform such a span themselves (`read_across_regions` /
  `write_across_regions` in `control::push16`, `pop16`, the ENTER display read and
  the IVT reads), as the walk did at 060d446a. The reviewer's alternative (store
  all three words, then commit SP once) would change the exit state of a frame
  that meets a gap part-way, so it was not taken.
- When the re-execution stopped at the first attempt's address with a host fault
  (the operand's first byte host-protected), the first attempt's guest-map exit
  was returned. The first exit is now kept only when the re-execution's exit is
  itself a guest-map containment miss; otherwise the re-execution's exit, with its
  HostAccess evidence, is returned.

Evidence (MEASURED): scratch harnesses compared main at 6614d2e0, which carries
060d446a, with this tree. They covered INT 21h, INT3, far CALL, PUSHA, ENTER,
PUSHF, IRET, RETF, POPA and POPF on Alias, I286, I386, I8086 and V30, under `run`
and `run_steps`, with every frame word in turn meeting the edge. Layouts:
contained, RAM|RAM, gap on either side, Trap on either side, ROM on either side,
an IVT entry over a RAM|RAM edge, a gap or a Trap byte, and a host-protected page
part-way through the frame (contained and over a region edge at three offsets).
All 10,200 + 2,560 outcomes are equal, including exit, fault metadata, SP and the
other registers, flags, frame bytes, retirement and step counts. Host addresses
were normalised.

New permanent witnesses: in `cross_region_ram.rs`, module `reexecution`, an INT
frame, every stack-frame helper, Real16 block accounting (a mid-block split and
REP MOVSW, `run_steps` budgets 1–9) and Long64 block accounting (mid-block scalar
and 16-byte splits on every tier, with and without the Trap window); in
`cross_region_host_fault.rs`, a protected first byte (load and store, every tier,
as the first instruction and after an INC). With the two fixes reverted, the INT
frame and the protected first-byte witnesses fail; the others pass on both
(MEASURED).

### Round 1 against f15060b9: measurement method — MEASURED (history)

- Sides: the commit before xj-ir6.12 (f15060b9, the gate base), the commit that
  landed it (060d446a), and the candidate (060d446a plus this change). Each side is
  built from its own source snapshot into its own target directory, with the release
  profile as committed, `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=4`, the same toolchain and the same host
  (Apple M5 Pro, macOS, aarch64). Two builds of f15060b9 from different source paths
  give identical binaries.
- `real16_loops 1 3 <workload>` for every workload and every path: host
  instructions per guest step, the median of three rounds. The three sides run one
  after another for each workload, and the order rotates between workloads.
- Store probe: the instrumented copy of `interpreter_store_probe` described above
  (200000 iterations × 16 stores). Three rounds; in each round every cell runs once
  per side, in rotating side order.
- One benchmark process at a time. The formatted tree of this change produces the
  same `__text` bytes as the measured candidate.

## C4b NEC V20/V30 native-mode extensions (baseline 29ddeda9)

**Change (READ).** The NEC native-mode extensions (TEST1/CLR1/SET1/NOT1,
ROL4/ROR4, INS/EXT, ADD4S/SUB4S/CMP4S, REPC/REPNC, FPO2; spec.md §17.10) on the
twenty-bit path. What every model runs:
- the twenty-bit fetch is generic over the NEC entries: `fetch::<false>`
  (I8088/I8086) keeps the C2 byte loop, with the BRKEM branch compiled out;
  `fetch::<true>` (V20/V30) adds the 64h/65h prefixes and the cold
  `vm::i8086::nec_form` and `carry_repeat`. `resolve` picks the instantiation
  once per lifted instruction and carries a small `NecForm` in place of the
  BRKEM byte;
- the shared 8086 lowering `lift::i8086::instruction` only initialises the new
  `WrappedString::carry` field; the new lowering `lift::i8086::nec_form` is out
  of line (`#[inline(never)]`), like C4a's `nec_instruction`;
- the shared walk sends three more `LegacyOp` variants to the C4a NEC arm and
  passes `carry` to `interp::i8086::string`; `string_run_specialized` takes a
  `carry` argument that only the twenty-bit (`WRAPPED`) instantiations read,
  in the REPE/REPNE exit test.

**MEASURED result: the gate is met in every cell.**
- The 117 non-SMC cells are within +0.5 % of 29ddeda9 (median of three
  processes per side). The worst is `rep-movsw` on the twenty-bit paths, at
  +0.268 % to +0.302 %; the other twenty-bit cells are −0.243 % to +0.066 %,
  the alias −0.268 % to +0.011 % and the four system paths −0.082 % to
  +0.039 %.
- The 27 SMC cells (median of 15 processes per side) are −0.705 % to +0.292 %.
  The twenty-bit ones are −0.054 % to −0.006 % on I8088/I8086 and +0.190 % to
  +0.292 % on V20/V30.
- The store probe is −0.021 % to +0.216 % (worst: width 8, single, recovery on).

**Same-mode view of the twenty-bit SMC cells (MEASURED).** In this campaign the
base took one value in all 15 processes of every SMC cell. The candidate took
one value too, except `smc-next-64` on the four twenty-bit paths, where 1 of 15
processes sat 0.56 % to 0.60 % above the other 14. Against the base's single
value, the candidate's majority value is −0.037 % (I8088), −0.038 % (I8086),
+0.223 % (V20) and +0.220 % (V30); its single upper value is +0.566 %,
+0.538 %, +0.787 % and +0.786 %.

**What costs on the V20/V30 paths (READ / MEASURED).** On the SMC workloads,
which re-lift every step, V20/V30 run 2.09 % to 2.28 % above I8088 with this
change, against 1.84 % to 2.02 % at the base: `fetch::<true>` adds the 64h/65h
and 0Fh/66h/67h tests and the `carry` bookkeeping to every lifted NEC
instruction. `rep-movsw` on the twenty-bit paths follows the `carry` test in
the wrapped string loop: `string_run_specialized` for `WRAPPED` grows from
2,156 to 2,300 bytes (`nm`, `real16_loops`). Other sizes: the shared 8086
lowering `lift::i8086::instruction` is 8,864 bytes at the base and 8,828 here;
the NEC lowering `lift::i8086::nec_form` is a separate 3,356-byte function;
`vm::i8086::resolve`, with both fetch instantiations inlined, grows from 7,216
to 9,236 bytes.

**MEASURED method:** dev-shell Rust 1.98.1, macOS arm64 (Apple M5 Pro),
background QoS, four build jobs, release profile as committed. Base:
`git archive 29ddeda9` built in its own target (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`75ba8b76645e1fb85982778578dd801011155d80223cd40d1ae8b1464235afe4`). Candidate:
a source snapshot whose `x86jit-core` is identical to the delivered tree, built
in its own target (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`ac81f8c260a20fb9d1714a3ad5023bbbdb28f878933f70e30907efe29953e2f9`). For every
workload, literal `real16_loops 1 3 <workload>` (all nine paths), three process
pairs in one campaign, side order alternating between workloads and pairs; a
cell is the median over processes of each process's three-round median. The
three SMC workloads then got twelve more interleaved pairs (15 processes per
side). Equal steps and retirements are asserted for every cell. The store
probe is a counted copy of `interpreter_store_probe` (host instructions around
the measured run, 200000 iterations × 16 stores), built against each core
library, three rounds of the eight cells in rotating side order. One benchmark
process at a time; this round's test runs shared the host. Only instruction
counts are used.

### Round 1 and the SMC diagnosis — MEASURED (history)

Round 1 measured the first candidate (one non-generic fetch, a `CARRY` const
parameter on `string_run_specialized`; `real16_loops` SHA-256
`94531fc459f125b90ee00793b1919586d063f1d926b90fb752343d31a12cca78`) with the
method below, stopped after phase A and 7 or 8 of the 15 SMC processes per side:
- all 117 non-SMC cells within +0.5 % (worst +0.080 %, `straight-line` I386);
  the twenty-bit paths at −0.21 % to +0.00 %, the alias at −0.60 % to −0.01 %;
- the twenty-bit SMC cells +0.82 % to +1.01 % (median of 7 or 8 processes). The
  candidate took two values per process, 0.35 % to 0.41 % and 0.85 % to 0.89 %
  above the base, which took one;
- the alias and system-path SMC cells −0.22 % to +0.03 %.

Diagnosis, `smc-*` only, four interleaved processes per side, median of
processes (host instructions per step, change against 29ddeda9):

| Workload | Path | First candidate | Base fetch (V1) | Generic fetch (V2) |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `smc-next-1` | I8088 | +0.41 % | +0.67 % | +0.07 % |
| `smc-next-1` | V20 | +0.43 % | +0.71 % | +0.32 % |
| `smc-next-64` | I8088 | +0.46 % | +0.48 % | +0.07 % |
| `smc-next-64` | V20 | +0.56 % | +0.59 % | +0.36 % |
| `smc-rmw-64` | I8088 | +0.51 % | +0.38 % | +0.60 % |
| `smc-rmw-64` | V20 | +0.59 % | +0.48 % | +0.92 % |
| `smc-next-64` | Alias | +0.36 % | +0.35 % | −0.11 % |
| `smc-rmw-64` | Alias | −0.02 % | −0.02 % | −0.68 % |

V1 is the first candidate with `vm/i8086.rs` replaced by the base file, so the
I8088 lift runs the base fetch and resolve source; it is as far from the base
as the first candidate. V2 makes the fetch generic, so the I8088 instantiation
has no NEC branch; it moves the cells by −0.4 % to +0.3 % against the first
candidate, in both directions. The I8088 path's lifted IR, decode and walk ops
for these workloads are the same in all three (READ, diff). The symbol tables
(`nm`, `real16_loops`) show the codegen-unit partition moving between the
builds: `LiftSource::validate` is 460, 760, 460 and 1,012 bytes in the base,
the first candidate, V1 and V2; the `time_stats` TLS closures are inlined in
the first candidate only; two `TranslationCache::insert_checked` instances grow
from about 1,600 to about 4,450 bytes in V2. The SMC cells follow layout, as in
C4a and xj-ir6.31 (ASSUMED as the mechanism; no counter attributes it).
The final candidate is V2 with the REPC/REPNC test as a run-time argument
instead of a const parameter (V3); its SMC-path symbols have V2's sizes.

**MEASURED validation (delivered tree):** `cargo fmt --check`, Clippy (all
targets and features, warnings denied) and the workspace release test check
with Unicorn are clean. 39 test binaries ran (`--test-threads 2`, 180 s
limit): every `legacy_*` binary (`legacy_nec_v20` 40/40,
`legacy_nec_v20_extensions` 20/20), `run_steps`, `irq_shadow`,
`retired_count`, `smc`, `same_block_smc`, `fences`, `fault_metadata`,
`cross_region_ram`, `cross_region_host_fault`, boundary/environment, the
Cranelift library and `application_codegen` witnesses, the core and
x86jit-tests libraries, and `region_fault_state` at default priority. In total
1,575 passed, 8 ignored and 0 failed.

one `UnsupportedSystemOperation` variant. Built from a snapshot whose
`x86jit-core` equals the delivered tree (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`c82b167e27b06cb75dd35fb017edef2e839a8c2cb66fd3e4a851a0a3e3654019`), measured

- Store probe, eight cells, three rounds: −0.001 % to +0.001 %.
- `smc-next-64`, 15 processes per side: the median is +0.29 % to +0.30 % on

The same cell against 29ddeda9, interleaved, 15 processes per side: median
−0.171 % (I8088), −0.174 % (I8086), +0.100 % (V20), +0.099 % (V30), −0.752 %
(alias), +0.029 % (I286): within the gate. The base took one value in 12 of

processes (+0.70 % on I8088/I8086 against 29ddeda9); its symbol tables differ
from the candidate's in 42 functions, none on the twenty-bit lift path except

bytes). The out-of-line test was kept.

## Read-modify-write and locked operands on MMIO (xj-ir6.33, baseline b2ac76e4)

### Change — READ

- **Replayed read.** An application walk (the Real16 alias, Long64, Compat32)
  consumed the `complete_mmio_read` answer in its first load and kept nothing.
  The retry after the `MmioWrite` acknowledgement of a read-modify-write then found
  no answer and stopped at a second `MmioRead` of the same operand (MEASURED at
  b2ac76e4 on the alias and on Long64/Compat32 at all three tiers). A walk with an
  MMIO retry open now loads through the out-of-line `exec_load_retry`: it consumes
  the answer as before, records it in the retry ledger (instruction and address),
  and takes the recorded answer when a later attempt's load stops at the device.
  Walks without an open retry, which are the ones the gates run, compile the
  unchanged `exec_load`. Compiled Long64/Compat32 code defers a Trap instruction
  to the interpreter, so the fix reaches every tier with no codegen change.
- **XCHG and LOCK on MMIO.** On Real16 and the Machine models, a locked or
  implicitly locked operand inside one Trap region is one `MmioRead`, then one
  `MmioWrite`, of the operand. The alias and Direct walks take the non-generic,
  cold `locked_device_operand` from the device arm of `exec_atomic_rmw` /
  `exec_atomic_cas` (XCHG, XADD, CMPXCHG, the LOCK ALU and bit forms); the Checked
  `AtomicRmw` arm uses the bus read and write it already had for operands that A20
  splits. A partly-device operand keeps `Exit::UnknownInstruction` (after review;
  the measured candidate reported the containment miss). Long64/Compat32 keep
  `Exit::UnknownInstruction` (`locked_bus_transfers`), and so does the alias's
  CMPXCHG8B.
- **A20-split stores.** A split store with a RAM piece and a device piece writes
  the device piece first (`split_device_follows_ram`, on the split path only), so a
  device stop leaves the RAM piece uncommitted.

### Intermediate candidates — MEASURED / READ

Intermediate candidates, each against b2ac76e4 with
`real16_loops 4 3 {alu,store,call-ret}` (one process per side, host
instructions per step):

| Candidate | Content | Worst cells |
|---|---|---|
| first | `exec_load` itself records and reuses the answer; generic device helpers with closures; `memory::apply_rmw` made `pub(crate)`; a CMPXCHG8B device path; the twenty-bit store writes device bytes first | +1.9 % twenty-bit `store`, +1.5 % twenty-bit `call-ret`, +1.1 % alias `load` (campaign stopped after two pairs) |
| A | b2ac76e4 plus the replay fix only | −0.00 % to +0.02 % on every path; 49 symbols differ |
| B | the locked device path as generic helpers with closures, `memory::apply_rmw` `pub(crate)`, the CMPXCHG8B device path | +0.5 % to +1.1 % on every path; 1,162 symbols differ |
| B2 | B with one non-generic device function | +0.45 % to +1.06 % on every path; 1,111 symbols differ |
| B3 | the delivered tree: A, the locked device path with private `apply_rmw`, no CMPXCHG8B device path, the A20-split store order | +0.12 % to +0.27 % on every path; 1,132 symbols differ |
| C | b2ac76e4 plus the twenty-bit device-first store and the A20-split store order | +0.62 % twenty-bit `store`, +0.35 % `call-ret` |

READ (symbol tables from `nm` address deltas, objdump call lists): B and B2 move
the codegen-unit partition. Their twenty-bit hot walk
(`interpret_block_inner<false,…,DIRECT20=true>`) grows by 456 bytes and gains four
out-of-line calls to the `HOST_FAULT_ADDRESS` thread-local closure. B3 still moves
the partition (`vload`, `BTreeMap` routines and lift helpers change inlining; the
same walk is 32 bytes smaller with three such calls). Its non-SMC and store cells
are within the gate, and eight twenty-bit SMC cells miss it (below). The uniform
+0.08 % to +0.24 % on the non-SMC cells is attributed to that layout change
(ASSUMED; no counter attributes it). D, B3 with the device function generic over
the walk's constants, measured the same as B3 (1,132 symbols differ). The twenty-bit device-first store (C)
was left out of the measured candidate for its +0.6 % to +0.8 % on the twenty-bit
`store` cells; the review restored it (P1-1, below).

### Generated code for Long64/Compat32 — MEASURED / READ

- READ: `x86jit-core/src/lift/`, `x86jit-core/src/ir*`, `x86jit-core/src/jit_abi*`
  and `x86jit-cranelift/` are unchanged against b2ac76e4.
- MEASURED: a measurement-only dump (`ir_emission_dump`, linked against each core)
  lifts 16 Long64 and 16 Compat32 blocks (ADD/ADC/INC/NEG/SHL on memory, XCHG,
  LOCK ADD/INC/BTS, CMPXCHG, LOCK XADD, CMPXCHG8B, MOV load/store, CMP) and
  compiles each with `JitBackend`. All 32 lifted IR blocks are identical. The
  emitted host bytes are identical once the MOVZ/MOVK immediates are masked; those
  carry run-time helper addresses and differ between two runs of the same base
  binary in the same way (4 of 32 blocks are byte-identical unmasked in both
  comparisons).
- MEASURED: `application_codegen` (3 tests: Long64/Compat32 block bytes, the
  Real16 lifted-IR fixtures, block/region/linked execution) and the Cranelift
  library tests, which expand the relocated application codegen snapshots, pass.
- Behaviour (MEASURED, `mmio_rmw::application_modes_read_then_write_once_at_every_tier`):
  an ordinary read-modify-write on a Trap operand now stops once at `MmioRead` and
  once at `MmioWrite` at the interpreter, block and region tiers; b2ac76e4 stopped
  at a third, replayed `MmioRead`. XCHG and the LOCK forms keep
  `UnknownInstruction`.

### Method — MEASURED

Dev-shell Rust 1.98.1, macOS arm64, release profile as committed. Base:
`git archive b2ac76e4` in its own target (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`c82b167e27b06cb75dd35fb017edef2e839a8c2cb66fd3e4a851a0a3e3654019`, the binary
the C4b review check measured). Candidate: a source snapshot whose `x86jit-core`
equals the delivered tree, in its own target (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`442680851cf3dd1e43ddf45f1bfe79f9e83ad4ff8c2183fee8919e32bdcd6630`). For each of
the 16 workloads, literal `real16_loops 1 3 <workload>` (all nine paths), three
process pairs, side order alternating between workloads and pairs; a cell is the
median over processes of each process's three-round median. The three SMC
workloads got twelve more interleaved pairs (15 processes per side). Equal steps
and retirements were checked for every cell (no mismatch). The store probe is the
counted copy of `interpreter_store_probe` (200000 iterations × 16 stores) linked
against each core, eight cells, three rounds in rotating side order. One benchmark
process at a time; builds and test runs shared the host. Only instruction counts
are used.

### Gate, SMC — MEASURED: eight twenty-bit cells miss

Host instructions per step over 15 processes per side; values within 0.3 % of
each other are one group, shown as median (count). The alias and the four system
paths are −0.14 % to +0.00 %. On the four twenty-bit paths both sides take one of
two values per process, about 0.6 % apart. Against the same value of the base, the
candidate is +0.29 % to +0.41 % (`smc-next-1`), +0.31 % to +0.33 % (`smc-next-64`)
and +0.18 % to +0.21 % (`smc-rmw-64`), all within +0.5 % (second table). The base
took its lower value in 8 (`smc-next-1`) and 10 (`smc-next-64`) of 15 processes,
the candidate in 4 and 6, so the medians compare different values: `smc-next-1`
+0.81 % to +0.83 % and `smc-next-64` +0.87 % to +0.91 % miss the gate on I8088,
I8086, V20 and V30; `smc-rmw-64` is +0.29 % to +0.31 %. READ: the twenty-bit SMC
workloads re-lift every step, and the candidate's codegen-unit partition changes
inlining on that path although none of its source changed: the `BTreeMap` insert
and entry instantiations used by `LiftSource::watch`, `FetchPolicy::approve` and
`Memory::mark_code` are split into different functions (`FetchPolicy::approve`
makes two such calls instead of one), and `lift_insn_inner` and other lift
helpers shrink by 176 to 340 bytes. The same-value increase is attributed to that
(ASSUMED; no counter attributes it).

### Remaining — READ

- A locked operand only partly in a Trap region keeps `Exit::UnknownInstruction`
  (review fix P2-3); performing it with separate exits is a follow-up.
- The twenty-bit models' device transfers are byte-wide; the 16-bit bus of the
  8086 and V30 takes an even-aligned word in one cycle. Bus-width-faithful
  transfers are a follow-up (spec.md §5.2).
- The alias's CMPXCHG8B keeps `Exit::UnknownInstruction` on a Trap operand; its
  device path was part of candidates B and B2.
- The pending answer of `complete_mmio_read` is still taken by the instruction's
  first load; binding it to its request is xj-ir6.13.

### Review fixes — READ

The campaign above measured the candidate before review. The review changed:
- the twenty-bit store of a word over a RAM byte and a device byte now writes the
  device byte first (P1-1): the device branch of the existing validation loop
  hands every store with a device byte to a cold function, which validates the
  rest and writes device bytes, then RAM bytes. At
  b2ac76e4 and in the measured candidate, the retry re-read the committed RAM
  byte: XCHG left AX 125Ah instead of 1277h, ADD left D52Bh instead of D5D1h
  (MEASURED); it now gives the result of the operand as first read in both byte
  orders (MEASURED, `twenty_bit_rmw_over_ram_and_device`);
- a recorded answer is bound to its request: the owner (RIP, CS) plus a
  fingerprint of the instruction's lifted operations, and a key holding the
  address, size and direction (P2-1). Code written at the same RIP between the
  exits makes the new instruction read the device itself;
- a recorded answer lives only across the instruction's own device stops: any
  other stop drops it and fault evidence does not report it, as at b2ac76e4
  (P3-2, Long64 and Real16 witnesses);
- the recorded answer is consulted before the pending one (P3-3);
- a locked operand only partly in a Trap region, including an A20-split one with
  a RAM piece and a device piece, returns `UnknownInstruction` again (P2-3).

### Recorded answers only at Trap (re-review 2, fix (a)) — MEASURED

`bound_answer` now also requires the access to still lie in a Trap region
(`Memory::region_kind`); a region remapped as RAM, or unmapped, between the exits
drops the answer. The check is in a cold function that the workloads below do
not run, yet its form moved the twenty-bit SMC cells: the same check as a
`mem.read` probe in `exec_load_retry`, or as a separate cold function, gave one
value per process on the twenty-bit paths, 19790.8 and 19812.4 host instructions
per step on `smc-next-64` I8088 (+0.77 % and +0.88 % against b2ac76e4, nine
processes). Folded into `bound_answer` (delivered, real16_loops sha256
`00885a86ad36d833d1fad9327443dac6cbb07b3e5d0452a754da60511dfedb08`) every cell is
within +0.5 % against b2ac76e4 (worst +0.284 %, `smc-next-1` I8086). The movement
is attributed to codegen-unit partitioning (ASSUMED, as above). Three builds
rotated per pair; three processes per build for `ip-boundary-1`, nine for the SMC
workloads.

## Codegen-unit regression after xj-ir6.33 (xj-ir6.36, baseline b2ac76e4)

### Finding — MEASURED

xj-ir6.33 as landed (f9aa0c98) costs +1.0 % to +2.2 % host instructions per step.
The cost is on the twenty-bit paths, on `alu`, `load`, `store`, `push-pop`,
`call-ret`, `dos-mixed` and `straight-line`. The system paths cost +1.2 % on
`straight-line` and +0.5 % on `push-pop`. The xj-ir6.33 gate above passed
because its review rounds measured only `ip-boundary-1` and the SMC workloads.

The step is 3bf14e3c, the budget and host stop change (re-review P1-2). It
touches only `CpuState::finish_mmio_retry` and adds `keep_only_bound_answers`.
Three process pairs per build:

| Workload | Path | b2ac76e4 | d59d181e | 3bf14e3c | f9aa0c98 |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `alu` | I8086 | 401.034 | 400.885 | 405.123 | 405.156 |
| `push-pop` | I8086 | 650.318 | 651.695 | 660.597 | 660.627 |
| `straight-line` | I8086 | 514.097 | 515.968 | 525.158 | 525.093 |
| `store` | I8086 | 542.782 | 544.398 | 549.135 | 549.075 |
| `straight-line` | I286 | 381.783 | 381.774 | 386.428 | 386.427 |
| `push-pop` | I286 | 489.305 | 489.418 | 491.806 | 491.886 |

The function grows from 220 to 320 bytes, and its direct calls go from 25 to 236.
Code whose source did not change also compiles differently. For example,
`vm::i8086::resolve` now inlines `ModelIsa::decode`.

The same function out of line does not recover the cells. Three variants stay at
+2.19 % on I8086 `straight-line`:
- a forced-inline fast test with a cold remainder;
- the same, also with the no-exit case inlined;
- d59d181e's body with an early branch to a cold boundary function.

Only `#[inline]` recovers them.

ASSUMED: the mechanism is rustc's codegen-unit partitioning in the default release
profile. A non-`#[inline]` function is code-generated once, in its module's unit.
Its growth changed how the units were merged, and with that the inlining in
untouched hot code. An `#[inline]` function is instantiated in each calling unit.
With it, `resolve` again matches d59d181e (2 normalized lines differ, 188 before).

The fingerprint is not the cause. `<IrOp as Debug>::fmt` has been linked since
d59d181e, through `instruction_fingerprint`. Only the cold bound-answer functions
reach it, from the retry walks. d59d181e measures at the base on the system paths.

### Change — READ

`#[inline]` on `CpuState::finish_mmio_retry`, with a doc comment that says why.
Behaviour is unchanged. The witnesses pass: `mmio_rmw` 23, `locked_mmio` 16,
`run_steps` 47 and `fault_metadata` 59 tests, and the 24 `legacy_*` binaries
that run on this host (`legacy_upper_audit` is Linux x86-64 only), 793 tests in
total.

### Method — MEASURED

Dev-shell Rust 1.98.1, macOS arm64, release profile as committed. Base: `git
archive b2ac76e4` (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`c82b167e27b06cb75dd35fb017edef2e839a8c2cb66fd3e4a851a0a3e3654019`,
`counted_store_probe`
`62c304ccfe61537c665881a4742e17d61914e512c4cf1bbdd3364baa0be2bf71`). Candidate:
80b54f8a with this change, built both from the worktree and from a source
snapshot; `real16_loops` is the same binary either way (SHA-256
`cce2f35a4afc1b94719f72766fb7e6d598254b057188fb7c0343c2d4fa4f4a0e`;
`counted_store_probe`
`de8dbf5cb63633402da034d703776eb938580f15ff032d5843e272e2eb9eeb23`).

The gate uses literal `real16_loops 1 3 <workload>` on all nine paths:
- three process pairs for each of the 13 non-SMC workloads;
- seven processes per side for each of the three SMC workloads;
- side order alternating between workloads and pairs.

A process value is the median of its three rounds. Equal steps and retirements
were checked for every cell. The decision-23 rule decides each cell. A side is
bimodal when its values split, at the largest neighbour ratio, into two groups
whose medians differ by more than 0.5 %. The store probe is the counted copy of
`interpreter_store_probe`, with 200000 iterations × 16 stores, eight cells and
three rounds. One benchmark process ran at a time.

## LOCK and the REP restart pair on the twenty-bit models (xj-50w.12, xj-50w.15; baseline b2ac76e4)

**Change (READ).** What every model runs:
- the twenty-bit fetch (`vm::i8086::fetch`) records a LOCK prefix instead of
  refusing it and pushes F0h into the bytes it gives iced only when one was
  seen (a test per decode attempt); `resolve` sends a locked instruction to
  the cold, out-of-line `lift::i8086::locked`, which keeps or refuses its
  lowered form;
- the shared 8086 lowering `lift::i8086::instruction` counts F0h as a prefix
  when it looks for an ESC opcode and turns the `AtomicRmw` of a locked
  instruction into `LegacyOp::LockedRmw20` through the cold
  `lift::i8086::locked_rmw`. The variant is the last of `LegacyOp`, so no
  existing discriminant moves, and `IrOp` stays 72 bytes;
- the shared walk sends `LockedRmw20` to the twenty-bit primitive arm, whose
  cold `interp::i8086::locked_rmw` calls `Memory::atomic_rmw`;
- xj-50w.15 adds no code to the dispatcher's per-block path. `run_steps` and
  `run` test whether a twenty-bit REP owner/restart pair is pending before
  their tail call to `run_direct20` (a load and a branch; the check and drop
  are the cold `forget_edited_rep_restart20`). At run exit, the twenty-bit
  engine tests the pair before it publishes the run's dispatch counts, and
  the cold `record_rep_restart20` records the string's bytes, its inputs and
  the registers. That engine no longer calls `finish_comparison_attempt`:
  only the protected-mode walk sets a REP flag origin (re-review fixes
  below).
No I80286, I80386, alias or Application lowering, walk or run code changes.

**MEASURED result: the gate is met in every cell.**
- The 117 non-SMC cells are within +0.5 % of b2ac76e4 (median of three
  processes per side). The worst is `ip-boundary-1` on the twenty-bit paths,
  +0.355 % to +0.380 %, against +0.152 % to +0.176 % on the system paths; the
  difference is about the run-entry test of xj-50w.15, which runs once per
  `run_steps` call (ASSUMED: rounds 7 and 10 below). Every other cell is
  −0.265 % (`rep-movsw`, twenty-bit) to +0.269 % (`call-ret`, I8086). The
  alias and the four system paths move by −0.104 % to +0.261 % with no change
  to their code (see the rounds below).
- The 27 SMC cells (median of 15 processes per side) are −0.469 % to
  +0.301 %; the twenty-bit ones −0.234 % to +0.301 %.
- The store probe is −0.234 % to +0.041 %.

**Same-mode view of the twenty-bit SMC cells (MEASURED).** Both sides take two
values per process, 0.5 % to 0.7 % apart. Split at the largest gap, the
candidate's values are +0.151 % to +0.402 % above the base's in the lower mode
and +0.351 % to +0.443 % in the upper one; the medians fall lower because this
campaign's base sat in its upper mode in 6 to 9 of 15 processes and the
candidate in 2 to 4.

**MEASURED method:** dev-shell Rust 1.98.1, macOS arm64, background QoS,
four build jobs, release profile as committed. Base: `git archive b2ac76e4`
built in its own target (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`c82b167e27b06cb75dd35fb017edef2e839a8c2cb66fd3e4a851a0a3e3654019`, the same
binary as the C4b review-fix check). Candidate: a source snapshot whose
`x86jit-core` is the tree before the review fixes, built in its own target
(`real16_loops` SHA-256 `4bc30ef0f7f324264661bb8c357dd858f5abc4c408e4040754bebb0ab665219f`). For every workload, literal
`real16_loops 1 3 <workload>` (all nine paths), three process pairs, side
order alternating between workloads and pairs; a cell is the median over
processes of each process's three-round median. The three SMC workloads then
got twelve more interleaved pairs (15 processes per side). Equal steps and
retirements are asserted for every cell. The store probe is the counted copy
of `interpreter_store_probe` (host instructions around the measured run,
200000 iterations × 16 stores) built against each core library, widths 8 and
32, three interleaved rounds of the eight cells. One benchmark process at a
time; other workers built and tested on the host meanwhile. Only instruction
counts are used.

### Rounds before the measured candidate — MEASURED (history)

Change in host instructions per guest step on the four twenty-bit paths
against b2ac76e4 (`real16_loops 1 3 <workload>`; rounds 0-7 one process per
side, rounds 7-10 two interleaved processes per side). The five other paths
moved alike in every round (−0.45 % to +0.36 %).

|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | pair inputs recorded in the dispatcher's post-block closure | +4.74 % | | | | | |
| 1 | LOCK only (base `vm/legacy.rs`) | +0.00 % | +1.17 to +1.24 % | +0.16 to +0.21 % | −0.06 to +0.02 % | +0.03 to +0.07 % | −0.06 to −0.00 % |
| 2 | 1 + out-of-line input record per string walk | | +1.11 to +1.16 % | +0.39 to +0.47 % | +0.45 to +0.51 % | +0.49 to +0.53 % | +0.03 to +0.08 % |

| 5 | run-entry and run-exit hooks inside the inlined engine | +0.71 to +0.74 % | +1.09 to +1.12 % | +0.68 to +0.76 % | +0.50 to +0.51 % | +0.97 to +1.01 % | +0.12 to +0.17 % |
| 6 | `Memory::atomic_rmw`, wrapped word refused; hooks around an out-of-line engine | +0.13 % | −0.33 to −0.30 % | +0.07 to +0.13 % | +0.05 to +0.07 % | +0.14 to +0.18 % | +1.32 to +1.39 % |
| 7 | 6 with the hooks at the `run_steps`/`run` call sites | +0.12 to +0.15 % | −0.29 to −0.26 % | +0.08 to +0.16 % | +0.06 to +0.08 % | +0.14 to +0.24 % | +0.57 to +0.66 % |
| 8 | exit hook in the engine, entry flag in `Vcpu` | +0.29 to +0.31 % | | | | +0.52 to +0.53 % | +0.40 to +0.42 % |
| 9 | 8 without the `Vcpu` flag | +0.30 to +0.33 % | −0.28 to −0.26 % | +0.34 to +0.37 % | +0.36 to +0.37 % | +0.53 to +0.54 % | +0.47 to +0.51 % |
| 10 | entry test before the tail call, exit record in `finish_comparison_attempt`'s cold path | +0.10 to +0.12 % | −0.31 to −0.28 % | +0.16 to +0.18 % | +0.17 to +0.18 % | +0.25 to +0.26 % | +0.37 to +0.40 % |

Round 10 went through the gate campaign (three process pairs, then the SMC
repeats; stopped after 3 or 4 SMC pairs). Its 117 non-SMC cells and the store
probe were within the gate (worst +0.387 %, `ip-boundary-1` I8086; store
probe −0.231 % to +0.046 %), but every twenty-bit SMC cell missed it:
`smc-next-1` +0.48 % to +0.53 %, `smc-next-64` +0.56 % to +0.59 %,
`smc-rmw-64` +0.93 % to +0.97 % (median of 3 or 4 processes). Those
workloads lift every step. The fetch built the bytes it gives iced with
`bytes.extend(lock.then_some(0xf0))` on every incremental decode attempt;
`if lock { bytes.push(0xf0) }` (round 11, the delivered tree) took about 80
host instructions per step off `smc-rmw-64` on I8088 (four processes:
20,440 to 20,466 against round 10's 20,513 to 20,611 and the base's 20,389 to
20,537).

What the rounds showed (MEASURED / READ):
- Round 0's post-block closure grew `run_direct20` by about 2,000 instructions
  (`llvm-objdump`); every twenty-bit block paid for it.
- Round 1's `rep-movsw` cost came with the LOCK helper calling
  `Memory::atomic_rmw_mode` and the one-byte transfer functions:
  `string_run_specialized`'s twenty-bit instances now called the
  `HOST_FAULT_ADDRESS` TLS accessor (5 call sites) and `i8086::string_load`
  grew from 218 to 247 instructions. Calling the public `Memory::atomic_rmw`
  and refusing a wrapped word (round 6) removed it.
- Any change inside the inlined engine of `run_direct20` (rounds 2, 5, 8, 9)
  moved the twenty-bit loops by 0.2 % to 1 %, although the added code runs
  once per run; outside it (round 7) a hook after `run_direct20` cost the
  tail call from `run_steps` (16 instructions per call, `ip-boundary-1`).
- From round 6 on, the other paths moved by +0.10 % to +0.25 % as well, with
  their code unchanged; 535 of 5,016 functions differ between the base and
  the candidate binaries (`llvm-objdump`), among them
  `TranslationCache::get_for_dispatch` (468 → 520 instructions). This is the
  codegen-unit partition effect C4a and xj-ir6.31 recorded (ASSUMED as the
  mechanism; no counter attributes it).

**MEASURED validation (delivered tree):** `cargo fmt --check`, Clippy (all
targets and features, warnings denied) and the workspace release test check
with Unicorn are clean. 38 test binaries ran (`--test-threads 2`, 180 s
limit; `region_fault_state` at default priority): every `legacy_*` binary
(`legacy_i8086` 59/59, `legacy_nec_v20_extensions` 32/32, `legacy_nec_v20`
40/40), `run_steps`, `irq_shadow`, `retired_count`, `smc`, `same_block_smc`,
`fences`, `fault_metadata`, `cross_region_ram`, `cross_region_host_fault`,
boundary/environment, the Cranelift `application_codegen` witnesses and the
core library (432). In total 1,400 passed, 4 ignored and 0 failed.

### Review fixes — MEASURED quick check

The review fixes (spec.md §17.10) add: the run entry's code-write drain and
block check (cold, once per run with a pair pending); the skip of the entry
test for zero-budget calls (a budget compare before it); the odd-address
refusal in the cold `interp::i8086::locked_rmw`; and the NEC CLR1/SET1/NOT1
atomic lowering (cold, out of line). Built from a snapshot whose
`x86jit-core` equals the review-fix tree (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`ad5ec401e78978ddec0f9db8808f50839e9771b2044eeeb8ba68f0d490f4e245`), measured
interleaved against the campaign's candidate binary (4bc30ef0…): three process
pairs of `real16_loops 1 3` for `alu`, `rep-movsw`, `push-pop` and
`ip-boundary-1`, 15 for `smc-next-64`, and three rounds of the store probe at
widths 8 and 32. Every cell is within −0.306 % to +0.319 % of the campaign
candidate. `rep-movsw` on the twenty-bit paths is +0.270 % to +0.282 %, which
returns it to the base: the campaign candidate was −0.248 % to −0.265 % there.
`smc-next-64` takes two values per process on both sides; on I8088 the
review fixes give 19,674–19,682 (9 processes) and 19,764–19,766 (6), the
campaign candidate 19,665–19,715 (10) and 19,823–19,842 (5).

### Re-review fixes — MEASURED quick check

The re-review fixes (spec.md §17.10) key the REP restart pair on the
instruction's bytes in guest memory instead of the translation cache. When a
twenty-bit run returns with a pair pending, the engine records the string's
bytes, its inputs and the registers in the cold `record_rep_restart20`,
before it publishes the run's dispatch counts. The next run's entry compares
them in the cold `forget_edited_rep_restart20`, which no longer drains code
writes or looks up the cache. The twenty-bit engine no longer calls
`finish_comparison_attempt`, because only the protected-mode walk sets a REP
flag origin. That removes the exit test the review-fix tree added.

Built from a snapshot whose `x86jit-core` equals the delivered tree
(`real16_loops` SHA-256
`b09ecc547fe8c87fe8cb795e4636501045814dd83aebf13b0851fec66104f753`). Three
binaries were run interleaved: b2ac76e4 (c82b167e…), the review-fix tree
(ad5ec401…) and this tree. Each ran three processes of `real16_loops 1 3` for
`rep-movsw`, `push-pop` and `ip-boundary-1`, and 15 processes for
`smc-next-64`.

Every cell is within −0.280 % to +0.183 % of b2ac76e4. Against the review
fixes the range is −0.440 % to +0.291 %. The largest increase is on the
alias path, whose code is unchanged.

`smc-next-64` takes two or three values per process on each side. On I8088,
b2ac76e4 gives 19,617–19,655 (8 processes) and 19,730–19,753 (7); this tree
gives 19,674–19,680 (9), 19,697 (2) and 19,720–19,721 (4).

Three earlier formulations were measured and rejected:
- Passing the VM to `finish_comparison_attempt` gave `ip-boundary-1` +0.63 %
  and `smc-next-64` +0.47 % to +0.77 % against b2ac76e4. The VM stayed live
  past the dispatch counts, and `run_direct20`'s register allocation changed.
- Recording before the dispatch counts while keeping
  `finish_comparison_attempt` gave `ip-boundary-1` +0.63 % and `smc-next-64`
  +0.61 % to +0.88 %.
- The second with a fixed 16-byte record in place of the byte vector,
  screened with three process pairs against the review fixes, moved
  `ip-boundary-1` and `smc-next-64` as the second did.
In every variant, as in this tree, `TranslationCache::get_for_dispatch` has
520 instructions against the base's 468, and 700 to 730 of about 5,060
functions differ from the review-fix binary. This is the codegen-unit partition effect
recorded above (ASSUMED as the mechanism).

Re-review 2 (MEASURED quick check, `real16_loops 1 3`, three interleaved
process pairs against the binary above): the recorded bytes are now the ones
the string's block was lifted from, carried by `LegacyOp::WrappedString`
(`code`, `code_len`), so the run exit reads no guest memory. On I8086,
`ip-boundary-1` changes by +0.001 % (2,390.400 → 2,390.422) and `rep-movsw` by
−0.561 % (357.119 → 355.114); I8088 and V20 move alike (`real16_loops`
SHA-256 `b84ecfabc03109698c75f28b1b5c03f38efc64a520d81cf823de9611333f3dd9`).

## I80286 quirks through the catalogue, xj-50w.11 and xj-50w.13 (baseline b2ac76e4)

**Change (READ).** The 80286 address width, LOCK forms and coprocessor
overrun, and the Direct length limit (spec.md §17.10). What the measured
paths run:
- the physical-bus gate (`legacy::physical_span`) takes the model's address
  mask (`legacy::physical_mask`, read from the catalogue in const blocks)
  instead of a fixed 32-bit wrap. The legacy walk's `LegacyAccess` carries
  the mask, set where it is built: once per Direct helper block, once per
  Checked instruction and once per shared helper op (the alias passes
  `u32::MAX`, its decision-23 §6 formation). Task-switch stages take an
  `AddressKeyed` view with the same mask. The dispatcher computes the mask
  once per call. The generic Direct walk forms no physical address through
  the gate;
- the Checked fetch (`vm::legacy::fetch_bytes`) is instantiated per model
  with the catalogue's mask and length limit as const parameters;
- LOCK MOV/MOVS/INS/OUTS, the coprocessor-overrun check and the Direct length
  limit are lift-time changes on the cold resolve paths
  (`lift::legacy::instruction`, `resolve_direct_uncached`).

**MEASURED method:** dev-shell Rust 1.98.1, macOS arm64, background QoS,
release profile as committed. Base: `git archive b2ac76e4` built in its own
target (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`c82b167e27b06cb75dd35fb017edef2e839a8c2cb66fd3e4a851a0a3e3654019`, the C4b
delivered binary). Candidates: the reviewed tree, v9 (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`c5d167b3a249869bd2003dd965787b53cdd2955e619faf4530ff653a5e2d07be`), and the
final tree after the review fixes, v10 = 1e6a9ec7 (`real16_loops` SHA-256
`942ef39239d9309b2c2634ba46d30ba9ef274030f39644feb16f5ea4c09e82e5`), each
built from a `git archive` snapshot in its own target. Literal
`real16_loops 1 3 <workload>` for every workload (all nine paths), side order
alternating between workloads and pairs, one benchmark process at a time; a
cell is the median over processes of each process's three-round median;
equal steps and retirements are asserted. Three process pairs for the
non-SMC workloads, fifteen per side for the SMC ones. The store probe is a
counted copy of `interpreter_store_probe` (host instructions around the
measured run, 200000 iterations × 16 stores), three interleaved rounds. Only
instruction counts are used; another worker shared the host.

### Final tree v10 (1e6a9ec7) — MEASURED, accepted by the maintainer

**Review fixes against v9 (MEASURED, interleaved):** `alu`, `call-ret`,
`ip-boundary-1`, `ip-boundary-64` and `store` on all nine paths, three
process pairs: −0.044 % to +0.057 %. Store probe, three rounds: −0.059 % to
+0.038 %. `smc-rmw-64`, fifteen processes per side: system paths +0.070 % to
+0.125 %, the four twenty-bit paths +0.415 % to +0.429 %, the alias +0.425 %.

**Direct SMC campaign, v10 against b2ac76e4 (MEASURED):** fifteen processes
per side, interleaved, one benchmark at a time, both binaries rebuilt from
`git archive` snapshots (base SHA-256 `c82b167e…` as before, v10
`942ef392…`). Cells over +0.5 % by the median of processes, all on the
twenty-bit paths:
- `smc-next-64`: I8088 +0.883 %, I8086 +0.882 %, V20 +0.853 %, V30 +0.853 %;
- `smc-rmw-64`: I8088 +0.728 %, I8086 +0.726 %, V20 +0.704 %, V30 +0.704 %.

The alias cells, unimodal, are +0.379 % (`smc-next-1`), +0.448 %
(`smc-next-64`) and +0.435 % (`smc-rmw-64`, the gate value for that cell);
the system paths are −0.026 % to +0.393 %. The twenty-bit cells take two
values per process on both sides. Their majority values differ by +0.855 %
to +0.891 % (`smc-next-64`) and +0.916 % to +0.946 % (`smc-rmw-64`);
like modes differ by about +0.31 % (`smc-next-64`, both modes) and +0.28 %
to +0.50 % (`smc-rmw-64`), and v10 sits in the upper mode in 9 to 11 of 15
processes where the base sits in the lower one in 9 to 10.

**Cause (ASSUMED, not attributed):** none of the v10 changes runs on the
twenty-bit or alias paths: the Direct refusal bytes, the Direct length cap and
the `#MF`-before-overrun order are on the cold system-model resolve and x87
paths, and the twenty-bit bus mapping is not reached there. The cells move as
the C4a and xj-ir6.31 SMC cells moved, with codegen layout. Three variants,
each reverting one of the three changes, were built but not measured: the
maintainer accepted the result first and stopped the attribution.
