| `smc-rmw-64` | I386 | 4773.6 (15) | 4792.4 (15) | 4773.6->4792.4 (+0.393%) | 4773.6->4792.4 (+0.393%) |
| `smc-rmw-64` | I286 A20 off | 4931.0 (15) | 4947.0 (15) | 4931.0->4947.0 (+0.326%) | 4931.0->4947.0 (+0.326%) |
| `smc-rmw-64` | I386 A20 off | 4775.6 (15) | 4794.0 (15) | 4775.6->4794.0 (+0.385%) | 4775.6->4794.0 (+0.385%) |

### Rounds and attribution — MEASURED (history)

- Round 1, first candidate (`real16_loops` 8c9da873…): 117 non-SMC cells
  within +0.5 % (worst +0.143 %), store probe −0.021 % to +0.004 %; the SMC
  cells of the four system paths +0.43 % to +0.79 % (2–3 processes per side),
  about 27 host instructions per step. SMC pages run the Checked
  one-instruction path, whose byte-wise fetch and per-instruction access carry
  the bus mask.
- Attribution on `smc-next-64`, one or two processes per binary, I286 / I386
  host instructions per step against about 3699 / 3564 at the base: lift
  helpers out of line, 3727 / 3593; gate forced to 32 bits, 3714 / 3579; mask
  precomputed and read in const blocks, 3726 / 3592–3596; that with the fetch
  and Checked execute given base constants, 3704–3707 / 3568–3571; the fetch
  loop instantiated per model, 3697 / 3565.
- Round 2 (per-model fetch, `real16_loops` dc7e6b3f…) and round 3 (plus the
  maintainer's LOCK rule, 5eff3eaf…): non-SMC within +0.5 % but the system
  paths about +1 host instruction per Direct step (call-ret +0.45 %,
  ip-boundary-64 +0.42 %): the dispatch loop's per-block bus-mask select. The
  final tree computes it once per call. A layout variant with the fetch loop
  out of line measured worse (smc-rmw-64 +0.62 %) and was dropped.

## Short campaign protocol (xj-193.6, xj-193.9)

`scripts/perf-campaign.sh` (commands.md, "Performance campaign") replaces the per-campaign
scripts of the earlier sections. This section records how its protocol was validated: the
divisors, the replays of recorded campaigns in the default release profile, and the same
landings in the `perf` profile (release with one codegen unit for `x86jit-core` and
`x86jit-bench`). Host: the Apple-silicon workstation of the earlier sections, loaded by other
work (load average 10–12) throughout. Builds ran under background QoS with
`CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=4`; measuring at default QoS, four processes at once.

### Randomization off — MEASURED

A process started through `posix_spawn` with `_POSIX_SPAWN_DISABLE_ASLR` maps its images at the
same address on every start (a probe process started twice each way: the same address twice with
the attribute, two different addresses without it). The earlier sections' two modes per process in the SMC and `straight-line`
cells came from randomization (turnaround analysis of 2026-11-18, 10 of 10 runs in one mode).

### Divisors — MEASURED

`python3 scripts/perf_campaign.py validate-divisors` on the `perf` binary of `019a8517`, three
processes per workload and divisor, randomization off. Largest change of a path's median of
processes against divisor 1, in %:

| Workload | /4 | /8 | /16 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `alu` | 0.018 | 0.020 | 0.021 |
| `load` | 0.032 | 0.038 | 0.047 |
| `load-odd` | 0.009 | 0.016 | 0.026 |
| `store` | 0.035 | 0.032 | 0.029 |
| `push-pop` | 0.010 | 0.037 | 0.039 |
| `data-next-to-code` | 0.014 | 0.033 | 0.032 |
| `data-next-to-dense-code` | 0.018 | 0.035 | 0.021 |
| `call-ret` | 0.039 | 0.029 | 0.026 |
| `dos-mixed` | 0.020 | 0.057 | 0.131 |
| `rep-movsw` | 0.021 | 0.020 | 0.023 |
| `straight-line` | 0.147 | 0.115 | 0.034 |
| `ip-boundary-1` | 0.031 | 0.033 | 0.042 |
| `ip-boundary-64` | 0.011 | 0.041 | 0.108 |
| `smc-next-1` | 0.024 | 0.025 | 0.019 |
| `smc-next-64` | 0.024 | 0.057 | 0.022 |
| `smc-rmw-64` | 0.023 | 0.020 | 0.034 |

`straight-line` grows with the divisor instead of shrinking (one warm-up and three measured
passes per unit), so it keeps divisor 1, the length every earlier campaign measured. Every
other workload runs at divisor 8: at most 0.057 % from divisor 1, inside the process-to-process
spread of the earlier campaigns (p50 0.07 %). Divisor 16 moves `dos-mixed` and `ip-boundary-64`
by 0.13 % and 0.11 % and is not used. The validation run (divisors 1, 4, 8 and 16, three
processes each) took 4 min 55 s; the campaign itself does not run divisor 1.

### Replays in the default release profile — MEASURED

Each landing rebuilt from git in the default release profile and measured with the campaign at
ten processes per cell and side (`--profile release --processes 10`), against the recorded
campaign's raw data at divisor 1 with randomization on. The rebuilt `real16_loops` binaries are
byte-identical to the recorded ones (sha256 prefixes `039fea23` add161a9, `d54107c0` b2089624,
`87a487b4` cf32ec2e, `8183882d` f14e9d1c, `b968a839` 8e5bc441, `e2c80c52` 6a3c2a3f), so the
replays differ from the records in protocol only. "Δ difference" compares each cell's change
with the record's: the median-of-processes change, or, on a recorded two-mode cell, the like-mode
pair of the lower base mode.

| Landing | Record: cells over | Replay: cells over | Same cells? | Δ difference, pp: p50 / p90 / max | Cells > 0.25 pp | Two-mode cells in 10 processes | Measuring |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| xj-50w.23 (cf32ec2e → f14e9d1c) | 4 | 4 | yes | 0.017 / 0.047 / 0.225 | 0 | 0 | 220 s |
| xj-50w.23 against pre-NMI (8e5bc441 → f14e9d1c) | 14 | 12 | the record's 2 alias SMC cells are not over | 0.019 / 0.076 / 0.210 | 0 | 0 | 283 s |
| xj-0w3.8 (add161a9 → b2089624) | 1 | 0 | `smc-rmw-64` alias: +0.540 % recorded, +0.445 % replayed | 0.050 / 0.183 / 0.703 | 13 | 0 | 212 s |
| xj-0w3.8 + LOADALL (add161a9 → cf32ec2e) | 0 | 0 | yes | 0.019 / 0.051 / 0.140 | 0 | 0 | 189 s |
| xj-50w.20 (019a8517 → 6a3c2a3f) | 0 | 0 | yes | 0.022 / 0.125 / 0.250 | 1 (`straight-line` I8088, 0.25 pp, two modes in the record) | 0 | 177 s |
| NMI (8e5bc441 → d6028809) | 56 (amendment) | 57 | blocks, as recorded | — (no raw record) | — | 1 | 237 s |

- **xj-50w.23 and the 2026-11-17 integration** meet the acceptance: the same cells over the gate,
  every cell within 0.25 pp of the record, no cell with two modes in ten processes.
- **Against 8e5bc441** every cell is within 0.21 pp. The two cells the record has over and the
  replay does not are the alias `smc-next-64` and `smc-rmw-64`: +0.69 % and +0.53 % recorded,
  +0.48 % and +0.44 % replayed, 0.21 and 0.09 pp apart. A hard threshold flips such cells in
  either protocol.
- **xj-0w3.8 does not meet the 0.25 pp bound** on 13 cells: twelve SMC cells of the twenty-bit
  paths (+0.21..+0.36 % replayed against −0.36..+0.01 % recorded) and one `straight-line` cell.
  In that record those cells spread 0.3–0.6 % within a side over 7 processes without splitting
  into two modes, so their recorded medians sit anywhere in that range; the replay measures one
  fixed layout. Its one recorded miss is +0.445 % in the replay, under the gate.
- **NMI still blocks**, with 57 cells over (call-ret +1.51..+1.87 %, ip-boundary-64
  +1.25..+1.41 %). One cell of 144 had two modes in ten processes: `ip-boundary-64` on the V20,
  where one candidate process measured +0.55 % above the other nine in all three of its rounds. So
  a per-process mode can still occur with randomization off, rarely; the bimodal fallback covers it.
- `straight-line` ran seven rounds per process in these replays (commands.md). A first
  xj-50w.23 replay with three rounds had four `straight-line` cells with two modes in ten
  processes, at the same verdict and every cell still within 0.23 pp of the record; see "Two
  modes" below for the cause.
- Ten processes per side take 3–5 min; the default three take about 1 min.

### The same landings in the `perf` profile — MEASURED

Each landing rebuilt from git in the `perf` profile and measured with the campaign's defaults:
three processes per cell and side, divisor 8 (`straight-line` 1, seven rounds), randomization
off. "Record" is the recorded campaign in the default profile, re-analysed with
`perf_campaign.py analyze` (it reproduces each record's verdict and miss list). Path means are
the change of the geometric mean of a path's 16 cells; the twenty-bit paths are the I8088, I8086,
V20 and V30, the 80286/80386 paths the four `legacy-*` paths.

| Landing | Base → candidate | Record: cells over +0.5 % | `perf`: cells over +0.5 % | Path means: alias / twenty-bit / 80286-80386 | Measuring |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|---:|
| xj-50w.23 | cf32ec2e → f14e9d1c | 4 | **0** | +0.03 / −0.93..−0.95 / −0.22 % | 59 s |
| xj-50w.23 against pre-NMI | 8e5bc441 → f14e9d1c | 14 | 18 | −0.38 / −0.98..−1.00 / +0.40..+0.41 % | 60 s |
| xj-0w3.8 | add161a9 → b2089624 | 1 | **0** | −0.00 / −0.10..−0.11 / +0.01..+0.02 % | 75 s |
| xj-0w3.8 + LOADALL (2026-11-17) | add161a9 → cf32ec2e | 0 | 0 | −0.04 / −0.11 / −0.00..+0.01 % | 61 s |
| xj-50w.20 | 019a8517 → 6a3c2a3f | 0 | **6** | −0.05 / +0.34..+0.35 / +0.00..+0.01 % | 58 s |
| NMI (with xj-50w.22) | 8e5bc441 → d6028809 | 56 (amendment) | 45 | −0.42 / −0.04..−0.08 / +0.59..+0.61 % | 73 s |

- **Layout misses go away.** The four `ip-boundary-1` misses of xj-50w.23 (+0.53..+0.63 % in the
  record) are +0.24..+0.30 % in the `perf` profile, and the `smc-rmw-64` alias miss of xj-0w3.8
  (+0.54 %) is +0.01 %. With the C4a replay of the turnaround analysis (8 misses → 0), that is
  three landings whose misses were all layout.
- **Real regressions still block.** NMI misses on 45 cells, led by `call-ret` +1.89..+2.01 % and
  `ip-boundary-64` +1.45..+1.55 % on the 80286/80386 paths, and its 80286/80386 path means are
  +0.59..+0.61 %. Against 8e5bc441, xj-50w.23's main still misses on 18 cells: the part of the
  NMI cost that xj-50w.23 did not recover (`call-ret` +1.40..+1.43 % on the 80286/80386 paths).
- **One miss the `perf` profile adds.** xj-50w.20 (device words on the 16-bit bus) makes
  `rep-movsw` on the four twenty-bit paths +2.73..+2.74 % in the `perf` profile: 329.2 → 338.2 host
  instructions per step on the I8088. The two other misses are `smc-next-64` on the I8086 and V30
  at +0.50..+0.52 %; the other twenty-bit cells move −0.07..+0.49 %, and the twenty-bit path means
  +0.34..+0.35 %. In the default profile the same cell is 360.2 on both sides (+0.004 %, ten
  processes). So the one-unit build makes this loop about 9 % cheaper than the default build, and
  xj-50w.20 gives back nearly a third of that advantage; it is a codegen effect of the `perf` profile, not
  a cost the default build pays. A one-unit build has layout and inlining effects of its own; it
  has fewer, not none.
- **Repeatability.** Each landing was measured twice in the `perf` profile, the first time with
  three rounds for `straight-line` as well. Outside `straight-line`, the two runs' cell deltas
  differ by p50 0.009–0.023, p90 0.033–0.076 and at most 0.225 percentage points. Cells within
  about 0.05 % of the gate change sides between such runs (xj-50w.20's `smc-next-64` misses moved
  from the I8088 to the I8086 and V30).
- **Two modes.** With three rounds, `straight-line` showed one or two "two-mode" cells in 3 of 6
  replays: single processes 0.5–1.8 % off. The per-round samples show the cause: one or two of a
  process's three rounds perturbed by 1–5 %, which a median of three does not reject. Its
  measured run is about 90000 guest steps, against millions for the other workloads. With seven
  rounds, no cell of any replay shows two modes. The bimodal fallback stays for the case it
  recurs.
- **Measuring took 58–75 s** per two-sided campaign, store probe included, on the loaded machine.

### Replays through the two-profile rule — MEASURED

The maintainer's gate: a cell, or a store-probe cell, blocks only when it is over +0.5 % in both
the release and the `perf` profile; over in one profile only, it is drift and does not block.
No new measuring: `perf_campaign.py combine` on the two tables above, the release replay at ten
processes and the `perf` replay at three (seven rounds for `straight-line`). No store-probe cell
is over in any profile of any landing.

| Landing | Release over | `perf` over | Block | Drift | Verdict (exit) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| xj-50w.23 (cf32ec2e → f14e9d1c) | 4 | 0 | 0 | 4 | DRIFT (3) |
| xj-50w.23 against pre-NMI (8e5bc441 → f14e9d1c) | 12 | 18 | 7 | 16 | BLOCK (1) |
| xj-0w3.8 (add161a9 → b2089624) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | PASS (0) |
| xj-0w3.8 + LOADALL (add161a9 → cf32ec2e) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | PASS (0) |
| xj-50w.20 (019a8517 → 6a3c2a3f) | 0 | 6 | 0 | 6 | DRIFT (3) |
| NMI (8e5bc441 → d6028809) | 57 | 45 | 37 | 28 | BLOCK (1) |

- **xj-50w.23:** drift on the four 80286/80386 `ip-boundary-1` cells, release +0.57..+0.61 %,
  `perf` +0.24..+0.30 %. It does not block.
- **xj-50w.20:** drift on `rep-movsw` on the twenty-bit paths (`perf` +2.73..+2.74 %, release
  −0.00..+0.00 %) and on `smc-next-64` on the I8086 and V30 (`perf` +0.50..+0.52 %). It does not
  block.
- **xj-0w3.8** passes in both profiles.
- **NMI blocks** on 37 cells over in both: `call-ret` on all 8 Machine paths (release
  +1.51..+1.87 %, `perf` +0.80..+2.01 %), `ip-boundary-64` on all 8, `ip-boundary-1` on the four
  twenty-bit paths, and `store`, `load`, `load-odd` and `smc-next-64` on the 80286/80386 paths.
- **Against 8e5bc441**, main at f14e9d1c blocks on `ip-boundary-1` on seven Machine paths
  (release +0.83..+1.22 %, `perf` +0.53..+0.86 %). The profiles disagree elsewhere: `call-ret` on
  the 80286/80386 paths is +1.40..+1.43 % in `perf` and +0.04..+0.08 % in release, and
  `rep-movsw` on the twenty-bit paths +0.54..+0.57 % in release and about 0 in `perf`. Single
  cells move with the build configuration in both directions; the rule keeps what both see.

### Timing and a null comparison — MEASURED

- **A campaign with a cached base:** `scripts/perf-campaign.sh 6a3c2a3f <this working tree>`,
  base binaries in the cache, the dependencies already built in the cache's target directory:
  948 s end to end, of which 889 s is the candidate's `perf` build (background QoS,
  `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=4`, load average about 10, with two ten-process replays of this section
  measuring alongside for part of it) and 57 s is measuring. That is 48 s over the 15 min aimed
  for; the build is 94 % of it.
- **A two-profile campaign with cached bases** (the default since the two-profile rule):
  `scripts/perf-campaign.sh 6a3c2a3f <this working tree>`, both profiles' base binaries in the
  cache. Two runs: 1333 s (release build 533 s, `perf` build 703 s, measuring 47 s + 49 s; the
  cache's target directory had been deleted, so both builds also compiled the dependencies) and
  1734 s (646 s and 965 s, measuring 58 s + 63 s; dependencies cached, load average 14–15 from
  other work). The two candidate builds are 93 % of the wall time; measuring both profiles
  takes about 2 min. Both runs: PASS in both profiles, and the candidate's binaries are

- **Builds of one side** in this section: `perf` 659–889 s (the first, with all dependencies,
  794 s), default release 498–620 s; the one-unit profile costs about 20–40 % more build time.
- **Measuring:** 48–75 s for the default three processes per side, 189–283 s for ten.
- **A null comparison:** the working tree's `real16_loops` is byte-identical to the one of
  `6a3c2a3f` built with the profile given on the cargo command line, so the profile in
  `Cargo.toml` and the script's fallback for older commits are the same profile. Measured against

  ±0.013 %, the spread within a side is p50 0.054 %, max 0.231 %, and the verdict is PASS.


## Borrowed Machine device transfers — MEASURED (2026-11-25)

Decision-28 stages 2–3, `machine_exits`: I80386, interpreter, Fast consistency,
macOS ARM64, default Cargo release. Three fresh processes, three rounds each,
20,000 iterations per cell after 100 warm-up iterations, ASLR disabled through
`scripts/perf_campaign.py::run_process`. Exit and handler order rotates by round.
N is the total retired instructions per device-loop iteration; final HLT is
also asserted. Scalar IN/OUT and byte MMIO loads/stores use identical guest
bytes and answers. The Exit path uses the existing `run_steps` and explicit
completion APIs, with the benchmark's default MMIO completion budget of one.
The handler path borrows one `DeviceIo` for the run; setup and warm-up are
outside measurement. No delivery logging, periodic interrupts or cycle timing.

Each cell is **Exit → handler, M retired instructions/s**, median of nine samples.
These compare transfer handling within one candidate binary; the separate
no-device decision-23 campaign compares existing execution against its baseline.

| Mode | Transfer | N=10 | N=25 | N=100 |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| real16 | out | 40.72 → 61.40 | 69.37 → 92.45 | 107.51 → 117.13 |
| real16 | in | 40.04 → 60.83 | 69.69 → 91.06 | 108.15 → 119.42 |
| real16 | store | 23.11 → 40.75 | 45.23 → 70.19 | 91.23 → 107.51 |
| real16 | load | 22.80 → 38.95 | 46.28 → 67.66 | 90.26 → 109.14 |
| flat32 | out | 21.90 → 25.75 | 33.77 → 37.41 | 42.95 → 44.62 |
| flat32 | in | 21.13 → 25.93 | 34.62 → 37.08 | 42.27 → 43.22 |
| flat32 | store | 15.09 → 21.78 | 27.52 → 34.52 | 40.89 → 42.91 |
| flat32 | load | 15.61 → 23.04 | 27.18 → 34.15 | 39.43 → 45.33 |
| paged32 | out | 12.71 → 13.42 | 21.82 → 22.90 | 32.16 → 31.88 |
| paged32 | in | 12.51 → 13.91 | 22.14 → 22.98 | 31.91 → 31.86 |
| paged32 | store | 6.38 → 8.27 | 13.48 → 15.84 | 25.45 → 27.35 |
| paged32 | load | 6.85 → 8.60 | 13.67 → 16.34 | 25.43 → 27.45 |
| seg16 | out | 21.98 → 25.55 | 34.21 → 38.22 | 42.73 → 44.00 |
| seg16 | in | 20.56 → 25.12 | 31.77 → 37.42 | 42.01 → 43.49 |
| seg16 | store | 15.88 → 21.91 | 26.74 → 33.69 | 39.52 → 42.86 |
| seg16 | load | 15.11 → 21.89 | 26.20 → 33.62 | 39.69 → 42.83 |

Host instruction counts, measured with `proc_pid_rusage().ri_instructions`,
fall in every cell: port transfers by 4.7–22.9% in Real16, 0.9–10.0% in Flat32
and Seg16, and 0.8–6.2% paged; MMIO by 10.4–33.6%, 3.9–22.4%, and 4.7–15.7%
respectively. Wall throughput includes guest execution; ns/transfer is an
amortized loop cost. The handler's advantage decreases as ordinary instructions
between transfers dominate. Counts support the work reduction independently of
frequency/load variation. Paged port cells at N=100 have wall medians
0.2–0.8% below Exit, despite about 0.8% fewer host instructions.

Reproduce with `machine_exits 20000 3` in three fresh processes through
`run_process`; the CSV's `path` column distinguishes `exit` and `handler`.
`EXIT_COMPLETION_BUDGET` retains its existing optional larger-budget comparison.
Raw CSV/metadata are outside the repository; no `bench/history` files were
written. Measured binary SHA-256: `782fea2295d643b49723cb038770aeebc07bac95764f4e867b22fb1717ad45e5`.

The decision-23 campaign against `6289334b`, with both `release` and `perf`,
three processes per cell and side and the standard +0.5% combined gate, reports
**DRIFT (exit 3): zero blocking cells, eleven release-only drift cells**. The
largest is the I386 one-step entry at +1.045% in release and +0.068% in perf;
the remaining release-only cells are twenty-bit one-step entries, Application
call/return and IP-boundary loops, and the Application store loop. Store-probe
gates pass in both profiles, with no measurement problems. This is a drift
verdict, not a claim that every release cell improved.

The Nix launcher was blocked by a sandbox cache-write denial. The fallback used
Cargo on PATH (Rust 1.98.1), no compiler wrapper, LLVM's Darwin linker and the
same flags for candidate profiles. Both baseline profiles came from the campaign
cache. Reproduce with `scripts/perf-campaign.sh --build-qos default --cache CACHE
--out OUT 6289334b TREE`; retain both profile reports and `combined.json`.


## Interpreter ceiling study — MEASURED 2026-11-26

xj-uv7: where the Machine-profile interpreter spends host instructions per guest step, and how
much each planned speed item can remove at most. Tree `c2cc119e` (main `e21be823` plus the
segmented-class change under review), default release profile with
`debug = "line-tables-only"` added for symbols. Host: the Apple-silicon workstation of the
earlier sections, macOS 27, rustc 1.98.1 (the embedder copy built with its pinned 1.99.1).
Other work kept the load average at 15–46 throughout, so every conclusion below rests on host
instruction counts (`proc_pid_rusage` `ri_instructions`, as the benches report them) and on
sample shares. No wall time is used.

### Method — MEASURED / READ

- **Counts.** `real16_loops` at divisor 8, three rounds, one path per process (a throwaway path
  filter); `machine32_loops` at 100,000 iterations, three rounds, I80386; and a PC-class
  embedder's CPU speed-ceiling example, run in a copy of the embedder's tree whose CPU
  dependency pointed at this worktree, with a host-instruction count added around each
  10,000,000-instruction sample (three samples). The embedder's own tree was not built or
  edited; the copy is deleted.
- **Samples.** `xctrace` Time Profiler attached for 3 s (2,000–3,400 samples at 1 kHz) to a
  one-path process inside its measured loop. Each leaf PC was symbolized with its inline chain
  (`atos -i` against a dSYM) and assigned to a category by its innermost frames and source
  lines. Shares are of time, not of instructions. Where an experiment measures the same cost
  the two agree within a few points (block lookup: 14–20 % of samples, 12–16 % of host
  instructions).
- **Ceiling experiments.** Throwaway runtime switches, one relaxed atomic load per site, so
  that one binary measures every variant; each delta is against the same binary with every
  switch off. The switches cost +8 to +60 host instructions per step against the clean build
  (mostly the memo bookkeeping in the dispatcher), so percentages are of a slightly heavier
  step. Everything was reverted; no code change is left.
- **The experiments.** *REP-restart scan off*: skip the twenty-bit dispatcher's per-block
  `find_map` over the block's ops for a repeated string. *Static links*: an eight-entry
  direct-mapped memo of `(physical, Arc<IrBlock>, walk)` in the dispatcher, consulted before
  the shared cache, not filled by blocks that end in an indirect `Jump`; *RET target* is the
  extra gain when those blocks are memoized too. Both still clone and drop the `Arc`, as a
  linked successor would hold one. *SMC/dirty poll off*: skip the per-block `DISPATCH_DIRTY`
  load and `handle_smc`. *Exit-request poll off*: skip the per-block `EXIT_REQUESTED | NMI_EDGE`
  test (xj-193.2). *Per-instruction checks off*: `StepContext::boundary` keeps only its budget
  test and skips the pending-I/O, exit-word and IRQ tests at every `InsnStart`. *Dead flags*:
  the JIT's backward flag liveness applied at lift to the interpreter's IR, live-out all at
  block end, and once more with live-out none (an upper bound for "also at block exit").
  *One more op*: four extra `ReadReg` ops after every `InsnStart`, divided by four: the
  marginal cost of the cheapest op. *Protected scan off*: skip `execute_instruction`'s
  per-instruction `ir.ops.iter().all(supported)`. *Fast hash*: a multiply-rotate hasher in
  place of SipHash for the Checked, Flat32/Segmented and paged block and context maps. *Paged
  lane*: `register_effects` also accepts `SegmentedAddress` and `Flat32Address` with
  `access: Execute`, the near-target check of a JMP or CALL in protected code; measured both
  as a switch and as a plain build.

### Where the time goes — MEASURED (percent of samples)

The stepped real-mode walk (alias, twenty-bit, and the I80286/I80386 Direct class):

| Category | `alu` alias | `alu` I8086 | `alu` I286 | `store` alias | `store` I8086 | `push-pop` I8086 | `call-ret` alias | `call-ret` I8086 | `call-ret` I286 | `dos-mixed` I8086 | `dos-mixed` I286 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Op dispatch: walk loop, `match`, op fields | 21 | 17 | 17 | 17 | 16 | 17 | 16 | 13 | 11 | 15 | 17 |
| Register and temporary access | 14 | 13 | 15 | 6 | 5 | 12 | 6 | 5 | 5 | 14 | 13 |
| ALU results and flags | 13 | 10 | 11 | 14 | 9 | 12 | 5 | 9 | 5 | 12 | 14 |
| Memory access path | · | · | 1 | 9 | 15 | 17 | 10 | 13 | 11 | 17 | 18 |
| Control-transfer helpers | 2 | 2 | 2 | 2 | 2 | 1 | 3 | 1 | 3 | 2 | 2 |
| Instruction boundary: budget, pending state, exit word, `InsnStart` | 16 | 15 | 16 | 13 | 12 | 9 | 8 | 7 | 6 | 10 | 11 |
| Walk entry/exit, lockstep bracket included | 10 | 11 | 12 | 12 | 12 | 6 | 17 | 17 | 20 | 8 | 6 |
| Block lookup: shared cache, locks, `Arc` | 16 | 15 | 16 | 20 | 14 | 9 | 26 | 20 | 23 | 10 | 10 |
| Dispatcher loop | 4 | 4 | 6 | 5 | 3 | 2 | 6 | 6 | 10 | 3 | 4 |
| Block end: SMC/dirty poll | 2 | 2 | 2 | 2 | 2 | 1 | 3 | 3 | 4 | 1 | 1 |
| Block end: twenty-bit REP-restart scan | · | 8 | · | · | 7 | 11 | · | 7 | · | 6 | · |
| Other | 1 | 1 | 3 | 1 | 3 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 |

The protected and paged walks (`machine32_loops` `register` unless named, and the embedder's
ceiling cases):

| Category | real16 | protected32 | protected16 | paged32 | `segmix` paged V86 | embedder 286 real16 | embedder 286 protected16 | embedder 386 flat32 | embedder 386 paged32 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Op dispatch: walk loop, `match`, op fields | 15 | 5 | 5 | 4 | 3 | 16 | 4 | 9 | 5 |
| Register and temporary access | 15 | 6 | 6 | 4 | 4 | 16 | 5 | 9 | 3 |
| ALU results and flags | 13 | 6 | 6 | 4 | 2 | 10 | 1 | 6 | 3 |
| Memory access path | 2 | 2 | 2 | 4 | 9 | 4 | 8 | 3 | 4 |
| Instruction boundary | 19 | 1 | 1 | 1 | · | 16 | · | 1 | · |
| Walk entry/exit | 13 | 2 | 2 | 3 | 5 | 9 | 1 | 3 | 4 |
| Protected walk: per-instruction restart and IR scans | · | 56 | 55 | 46 | 33 | · | 13 | 46 | 21 |
| Block lookup: shared cache, locks, `Arc` | 13 | 6 | 4 | 7 | 6 | 13 | · | 4 | 4 |
| Block lookup: SipHash of block and context keys | · | 9 | 9 | 15 | 9 | · | 13 | 9 | 8 |
| Dispatcher loop | 6 | 4 | 4 | 3 | 2 | 9 | 7 | 4 | 2 |
| Block end: SMC/dirty poll | 2 | 2 | 2 | 1 | 1 | 2 | 1 | 1 | 1 |
| Decode and lift | · | · | · | · | · | · | 17 | · | · |
| Other | 2 | 2 | 3 | 7 | 25 | 7 | 31 | 4 | 45 |

- **Real mode.** No single cost dominates. The walk itself (op dispatch, register access, ALU
  and flags) is 27–48 %, the memory path up to 18 %, the per-instruction boundary 6–16 %, and
  the per-block overhead (walk entry/exit, lookup, dispatcher loop, polls, the twenty-bit scan)
  30–55 %, the high end on one-instruction blocks (`call-ret`: CALL, LOOP and RET each end a
  block).
- **Protected and paged.** `execute_instructions` and `paging::execute_ordinary` run each
  instruction as its own `execute_instruction` → `walk_instruction` → `walk_ops` call. Per
  instruction that is a `position` scan for the next `InsnStart`, an
  `ops.iter().all(supported)` scan (`register_effects` and `fast_instruction` on paged
  blocks), a checkpoint and task-switch preamble, a lockstep bracket, an executor test per op
  (`machine_specific`), and the exit-word and dirty-code tests between instructions. The block
  and context maps (`LegacyBlockKey`, `LegacyContextStamp`) hash with SipHash.
- **Embedder 286 protected16.** The 80286 never gets the Segmented class (`legacy.rs`: only
  `CpuGeneration::I386` qualifies), so its protected mode is Checked: one-instruction blocks,
  the instruction bytes fetched and decoded again on every hit (`Execution::resolve`, decode
  17 % of samples), a SipHash lookup, and the one-instruction walk. Its "other" is copies and
  allocation in that path.
- **Embedder 386 paged32.** The loop's near JMP lowers to a `Jump` after either
  `SegmentedAddress { access: Execute }` (non-flat code segment) or
  `Flat32Address { access: Execute }` (`flat-paged32`). `register_effects` lists neither, so that instruction takes
  `execute_with_retry`, the two-phase paged transaction, which clones `CpuState` twice
  (`interp/legacy/paging.rs`: the preview and `before_commit`). Those clones and their copies
  are 34 % of the samples ("other" 45 %). `machine32_loops` ends its loop with JNZ (`Branch`),
  which is listed: 1055 there against 2046 here.
- **Twenty-bit memory.** Every RAM byte goes through a one-byte transfer: a region lookup
  (`region_at`), an MMIO-part test (`CpuState::mmio_part`, not inlined) and a write
  observation per byte. That is the remaining `push-pop`, `call-ret` and `rep-movsw` gap to the
  alias.
- **SMC.** Twenty-bit `smc-next-64`: decode 13 %, lift 7 %, allocator (`libsystem_malloc`)
  24 %, cache insert and code-span maps (SipHash, page counts) about 10 %; it relifts a block
  of up to 16 instructions after every store. I80286 `smc-next-64`: decode 35 %, the walk 14 %;
  its retained SMC pages relift one Checked instruction.

| Case | 386 before → after | 486 before → after |
|---|---:|---:|

| `flat-paged32` | 2057 → 1105 (−46.3 %) | 2322 → 1301 (−44.0 %) |

After it the embedder's paged32 costs what `machine32_loops` paged32 costs (1098 against
1055). Whether a segmented or flat near target may skip the transaction (its CS limit check
can fault; the comment on `register_effects` says the walk keeps that check and its rollback)
is for the change to show; the measurement only bounds the gain.

### The planned items — MEASURED / ASSUMED

Gains are host instructions per guest step at most, from the experiments above; "ASSUMED"
marks a derived estimate.

| Item | Ceiling where it applies | Evidence | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| .27 link interpreted blocks | real mode: −12..−15 % (`alu`, `load`, `store`), −18..−20 % (`ip-boundary-64`), −8..−11 % (`push-pop`), −11..−15 % (`call-ret`, static targets), −5..−7 % (`dos-mixed`); embedder real16 −12 %. Protected and paged lookups are a different structure (see the hash below) | memo experiment; the shared-cache lookup costs about 190–220 host instructions per block (`code_lifetime` and shard `RwLock` reads, an `Arc` clone and drop of the lifetime and of the block, a hash probe) | high for the ceiling; a real link also needs an invalidation check the memo skipped |
| .26 cheaper op dispatch | the cheapest op (`ReadReg`) costs 25 (I80286/I80386), 28 (alias), 31 (twenty-bit) host instructions; ops per guest instruction 4.3 (`alu`), 5–6 (`load`, `store`), 6.5–7.5 (`push-pop`), 7.7–9 (`call-ret`), so dispatch and operand plumbing are 20–32 % of a step. With a compact pre-resolved encoding at about 10 per op (ASSUMED): −12..−22 % in real mode | one-more-op experiment; IR dumps; sample shares (op dispatch plus register access 16–35 %) | medium for the share, low for the post-change floor |
| xj-50w.16 twenty-bit cost over the alias | the per-block REP-restart `find_map` is −20..−70 per step (−5..−7.5 % on every twenty-bit workload). The rest of the gap is the byte-wise memory path: after the scan, I8086 over alias is +5 % (`alu`), +15 % (`load`), +19 % (`store`), +27 % (`push-pop`), +31 % (`call-ret`), +38 % (`dos-mixed`), +124 % (`rep-movsw`); SMC is +108 %, the 16-instruction relift with its allocations | REP-restart experiment; twenty-bit profiles | high (scan), medium (byte path) |
| .30 return-address prediction | `call-ret` −6.6..−8.7 % beyond the static links, `dos-mixed` −2 %, zero elsewhere; needs .27 | RET part of the memo | medium-high |
| .29 fused ops | LOOP is already one op (`LoopCx`); a near CALL is 8–10 ops, RET 9–12, PUSH and POP 6–8. Removing four to six dispatches per stack instruction at about 20 each (ASSUMED): `push-pop` −10..−13 %, `call-ret` −8..−11 %, `dos-mixed` −4..−7 %, `alu` about 0. CMP/TEST+Jcc fusion keeps its flag writes unless .28 proves them dead | per-op cost, IR dumps | low-medium |
| .28 live flags only, also at block exit | in-block liveness: −16 (`alu`, −4.4 %), −11 (`load`), −8 (`dos-mixed`), 0 (`store`, `push-pop`, `call-ret`); live-out none adds 0..−6 more. The interpreter computes flags eagerly per op and has no block-exit materialization step, so the earlier ~30 cycles per instruction at block exit does not apply to this walk. It must also recover exact flags at budget cuts and faults inside a block | dead-flag experiments | high; small |
| xj-193.2 per-block exit-request poll | 0..−5 per step (≤ 0.5 %) on every Machine and alias workload, the embedder included | exit-poll experiment | high; negligible |
| .31 cheaper decode/lift for cold and self-modifying code | steady loops 0. `smc-next-64`: twenty-bit 18,888 against 3,700 on the I80286, so relifting one instruction on SMC pages as the Direct class does could remove up to 80 % there; decode is 35 % of the I80286's SMC step | baselines, SMC profiles | medium |

### Not on the list — MEASURED

1. **The protected and paged walk restarts per instruction.** On the same five register
   instructions, real mode costs 338 and every unpaged protected class 819–823, paged
   1050–1055, embedder flat32 866. 33–56 % of the protected and paged samples (46–56 % of
   the unpaged ones) are the per-instruction restart (`execute_instructions` → `execute_instruction` → `walk_instruction`), its IR scans
   (`position`, `all(supported)`, `register_effects`, `fast_instruction`) and the per-op
   executor test. Proving `supported` at lift alone is −35..−91 per step (−4..−7.5 %). One
   pass over a proven block, as the real-mode Shared walk does, has a ceiling near the
   real-mode step: about −55..−60 % unpaged (ASSUMED from 823 → 338, less whatever checks
   protected mode keeps).
2. **SipHash on the protected block and context maps.** A cheap hasher on
   `Execution::{blocks, contexts}`, `flat32::State::blocks` and `paging::State::{contexts,
   blocks}` is −75 per step unpaged and −135 paged (−9 % / −12 %), −224 / −404 on `callret`
   (−13 % / −16 %).
3. **The 80286 has no Segmented class.** Its protected mode is Checked: one-instruction
   blocks, bytes fetched and decoded again on every hit, a SipHash lookup. The embedder's
   80286 protected16 ceiling costs 3045 per instruction, 8.1× its real mode. The I80386
   Segmented class runs the same kind of loop at 821–860, so extending it (decision-27 scope)
   is worth about −72 % there (ASSUMED from those two figures), more with items 1 and 2.
4. **The paged register lane misses near JMP and CALL targets in protected code.**
   `SegmentedAddress` and `Flat32Address` with `access: Execute` send the instruction through
   the two-phase transaction with two `CpuState` clones. Accepting them halves the embedder's
   paged ceilings (−44..−46 % on `paged32` and `flat-paged32`, −9..−11 % on its user-mode
   paged cases), the largest measured single change of the study.
5. **Per-instruction boundary checks.** `StepContext::boundary` tests the pending MMIO and
   port state, the exit word and the IRQ condition at every `InsnStart`: 19–22 per guest
   step on every stepped workload (2–6 %, −10 % on `rep-movsw`, where each iteration pays it).
   Deciding these once per block when the budget covers the block, and on the cold paths that
   change them, keeps the budget test only.
6. **Per-block SMC/dirty poll.** −6..−36 per step (1.3–3.7 %), the larger on one-instruction
   blocks. It is a load plus an out-of-line `handle_smc` call per block, separate from the
   exit-word test of xj-193.2.
7. **The lockstep bracket.** `lockstep::begin_with_trace` and the `Bracket` drop are 1.2–3.6 %
   of the samples on the alias and the I80286/I80386 Direct class and 0.1–1.2 % on the
   twenty-bit walk, with no trace configured.

### Recommended order

1. **Twenty-bit REP-restart flag at lift** (part of xj-50w.16): −5..−7.5 % on every
   twenty-bit workload for a per-block bool.
2. **The paged register lane for near targets** (new): −44..−46 % on the embedder's paged
   ceilings, −9..−11 % on its user-mode paged cases; a few lines, if the target check keeps its
   fault behaviour.
3. **A cheap hasher and lift-time `supported` for the protected maps and walk** (new):
   −8..−19 % on every protected, paged and V86 workload, the embedder's included; small and
   local.
4. **The Segmented class for the 80286** (new): about −72 % on 80286 protected mode.
5. **One pass over proven Flat32, Segmented and paged blocks** (new): up to about −55 % on all
   32-bit and V86 protected work, the largest remaining item for those regimes.
6. **.27 links, then .30** on top: −5..−20 % in real mode and a further −7..−9 % on
   call-heavy code.
7. **Per-instruction boundary checks once per block** (new): −2..−6 %.
8. **.26 compact dispatch**, then **.29** in the same encoding: −12..−22 % (ASSUMED), the
   largest real-mode item after .27 but the largest change.
9. **The twenty-bit word path** (rest of xj-50w.16): up to the +15..+124 % memory-path gap.
10. **.28**, the SMC/dirty poll, **.31** and **xj-193.2**: each under 5 % on these workloads
    (.31 matters only for self-modifying code; xj-193.2 is ≤ 0.5 % and can be dropped).

Items 1–5 are where the PC-class embedder's ceilings are lowest (80286 protected 3045, 386
paged 2046); items 6–8 lift real mode, which is already the cheapest regime.

## Interpreter flag liveness, xj-to9.28 — MEASURED 2026-11-26

Not applied: the gain is real on register-heavy real-mode loops, but computing it at lift costs
more than the +0.3 % limit on the self-modifying-code cells, and keeping the flags exact
needs more than lift-time narrowing. Tree `da16ccf0`, default release profile, host
instructions per guest step (`real16_loops` divisor 8, three rounds, all paths per process;
`machine32_loops` 100,000 iterations, three rounds, median), on a loaded shared host.
Throwaway switch, reverted; the switch costs one `OnceLock` load per lifted block.

The switch narrowed the ALU masks of the interpreter's IR (application `lift_block`, the
twenty-bit block assembly) with the JIT's backward liveness, all flags live at block end, in
three variants: **v1** block end only (the study's experiment); **v2** also all flags live
before every op that can leave the walk or observe state (anything but register, ALU,
`GetCond`, `SetCf`, `LAHF`/`SAHF`, `Jump`, `Branch`) and at the start of its instruction,
which is what exact flags at faults, MMIO exits, deliveries and rollbacks require, leaving
cuts at a plain `InsnStart` (budget, IRQ, TF, exit word, lockstep) to a cold recovery;
**v3** all flags live at every `InsnStart` (no recovery needed).

| Workload, paths | v1 | v2 | v3 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `alu`, alias / I80286, I80386 / twenty-bit | −4.5 / −4.4 / −4.1 % | same | 0 |
| `load`, `load-odd`, all paths | −2.0..−2.4 % | same | 0 |
| `straight-line`, alias and Direct / twenty-bit | −2.5..−2.8 / −1.6..−1.9 % | same | 0 |
| `dos-mixed`, alias and Direct / twenty-bit | −1.8 / −1.2 % | −1.8 / −1.0 % | 0 |
| `store`, `push-pop`, `call-ret`, `rep-movsw`, `ip-boundary-*`, `data-next-to-*` | 0 (±0.2 %) | 0 | 0 |
| `machine32_loops` `register`: `application-interp32` / `machine-real16` | −4.9 / −4.6 % | same | 0 |
| `machine32_loops` `memory` `application-interp32` | −1.3 % | 0 | 0 |
| every protected, paged and V86 path, every workload | 0 (±0.15 %) | 0 | 0 |
| SMC cells (`smc-next-1/64`, `smc-rmw-64`), alias / twenty-bit | +3.2..+4.3 / +1.7..+2.4 % | +3.8..+5.0 / +2.0..+2.7 % | +3.7..+4.8 / +2.0..+2.5 % |
| the same, allocation-free single-pass v2 | | +1.7..+2.0 / +0.34..+1.1 % | |

- **The gain** is cross-instruction only (v3 is zero) and survives the fault barriers (v2
  equals v1 except `dos-mixed` twenty-bit and interp32 `memory`). The protected and paged
  walks gain nothing: their IR is full of `Legacy` ops, which read all flags.
- **The cost** is the pass at lift: the SMC cells relift a block after every store, and even
  the allocation-free pass adds 70–190 host instructions per step there (I80286/I80386 0:
  they relift one Checked instruction). A shippable version adds a copy of the original masks
  on top, so eager narrowing at lift cannot meet +0.3 %.
- **What a version within the limit needs:** narrowing on a later hit of a block, not at lift
  (the SMC blocks run once), kept beside the architectural IR, not in it, because JIT tier-up
  takes `ir.ops` as the original masks for its own exit recovery and `retry_instruction`
  slices one instruction out of a block; plus recovery of the omitted flags at every
  `InsnStart` cut, from the writer's inputs (nothing checks that temps are assigned once, so that
  needs a check or a saved recipe). That is a new `IrBlock` member and changes in lift, dispatch and
  `walk_ops`, for at most the v2 column.

## Device access cost and the in-block handled path (xj-to9.23) — MEASURED (2026-11-25)

`machine_exits` (new cells: `planar`, a handled byte MMIO store every N=3/4/5
instructions; `unserved-out`, port accesses outside the one registered range,
with an `exit-noreg` path on a VM with no registration). Real16, interpreter,
Fast consistency, macOS ARM64, default Cargo release, 20,000 iterations, three
rounds, median. Host instructions per access (`proc_pid_rusage` ri_instructions)
are the reliable column; wall time was taken while another build ran.

Profile (`sample`, handled planar store, before): the first walk 25 %, the
dispatcher 23 %, the retry walk 8 %, translation lookups for the retry slice and
the suffix block 10 %, context stamps, `finish_mmio_retry` and the boundary
refresh most of the rest; `Run::access` with the registry lock 4 %. The unserved
port with a handler spent its extra time in the request construction, the lock
and the per-block device bookkeeping (port IP scan, boundary checks).

Changes: (1) registered ranges are a `Vm`-owned table (port bitmap, sorted MMIO
ranges) read without a lock, consulted before a request is built, and an
unserved transfer skips the in-dispatcher seal; the per-boundary and per-block
device checks run only with a deadline, INTR or queued invalidation; (2) a
handled scalar MMIO transfer of a real-mode shared walk completes inside the
block (spec.md §5.4).

| Model | Cell | N | base | final |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| I80286 | planar handler | 3 | 4874 | 1728 |
| I80286 | planar handler | 5 | 5371 | 2222 |
| I80286 | store handler | 10 | 6606 | 3457 |
| I80286 | store handler | 100 | 28839 | 25688 |
| I80286 | out handler | 10 | 4405 | 4314 |
| I80286 | unserved-out handler | 10 | 6419 | 6002 |
| I80286 | unserved-out exit-noreg | 10 | 5575 | 5602 |
| I80286 | store exit (no handler) | 10 | 9891 | 10002 |
| I80386 | planar handler | 3 | 4874 | 1728 |
| I80386 | out exit (no handler) | 100 | 27900 | 27894 |

Handled planar stores rise from 16.9 to 41.7 M retired/s (I80286, N=3). The
unserved port with a handler keeps 427 instructions (7.7 %) over no registration;
the Exit path pays 0.3–1.1 % for the Trap-miss check of the walk's memory
provider. The decision-23 campaign is not part of this entry.
